*Physical limits.* One camera beside the projector cannot measure the fingertip's distance from the wall, so no correction for arbitrary hover distances; within contact to ~5 cm the measured static error is ≤ ~10 px (mean 6–7 px). ~262 ms display latency is hardware. Palm lighting depends on the room light.

*Proposed changes, ranked (benefit / risk).*
1. Instrumentation (needed by every later stage; low risk): per frame in the `draw` trace, why a fingertip did or did not become ink (no hand / fingertip outside MediaPipe's view / mapped outside the canvas / shape not POINT / refused at stroke start / dropped in the toolbar band or under the panel / bridged by the pen-lift delay) and per-stage times (capture age, inference, shape, mapping, engine, controller, render, display); counters in the report. Files: `tools/draw.py`, `tools/tracking_session.py`, `interaction/drawing.py`; tests.
2. T1, toolbar trap (high benefit, low–medium risk; needs the user's choice): (a) a refused or interrupted stroke is visible: the cursor shows "lifted" (e.g. hollow ring) while a stroke cannot draw, so the user knows to open the hand; (b) a stroke entering the toolbar band ends there (recorded) instead of silently losing points; (c) option: move the toolbar to the top edge (or make it collapsible), so writing at the bottom and pressing buttons do not share the same strip. Not proposed: starting a stroke when the finger leaves the band while still pointing (it would draw the way from the toolbar to the writing as ink, the S1 lead-in problem).
3. T2, bottom losses (medium benefit, medium risk): (a) re-acquisition near the last-seen hand first (one of the 2 existing views per search frame; no extra inference, no threshold change); (b) a white (brighter) toolbar strip to light a palm over it (if the toolbar stays at the bottom). Judged on a fresh recording against the §9.12 criteria (bottom-band losses ≤ top/middle, no silent drops, re-find ≤ 0.6 s, fps not lower).
4. H2, hover steadiness (medium benefit, medium risk): tune the drawing path's fingertip smoothing for writing, chosen by offline replay of the recorded traces (jitter vs lag measured separately for the cursor and the ink); no change that adds ink lag beyond a stated limit.
5. Contact point (low benefit now): cursor = visible fingertip end; whether ink should sit at the fingertip pad needs its own protocol (a physical mark touched and held); later.
6. Hover geometry: no correction proposed (measured shift within the scatter); after the tape measurements, a predicted-parallax table for the operating range contact–5 cm.
7. Speed: no change before the stage timings; candidates then: AI process priority while tracking, the search budget when no hand is present.
8. UI (Stage 7, after 1–4): toolbar and canvas design with rendered mockups first; the AI unchanged.

*Tests.* Unit: the instrumentation counters; toolbar-band stroke ending; the lifted-cursor state; the re-acquisition order (`tests/test_acquisition.py`); smoothing replay. Hardware: B-3-style free writing near the bottom (losses, drops, re-find), the B-2 grid (contact / ~2 / ~5 cm) for steadiness, fps and AI runs.

*Expected impact.* Instrumentation < 0.1 ms per frame; T1 and T3 change no inference; smoothing changes cost nothing but may add or remove lag (measured); fps unchanged by design.

**Proposed criteria for Stage 4 (bottom tracking), awaiting the user:** on a fresh B-3 recording, hand losses in the bottom writing band (y 560–648) per 1000 hand frames no higher than in the top and middle bands; no stroke point dropped without a record; median time to re-find the hand ≤ 0.6 s; processing fps not lower than the baseline; no detection threshold loosened. Criteria for Stage 5 (hover) after the baseline, before any fix.

**User decision (2026-10-26): items 1–4 approved, to be built in order (1 instrumentation, 2 toolbar behaviour, 3 bottom-strip tracking, 4 hover smoothing), one at a time; the toolbar stays at the bottom for now.** Item 1 approved with conditions: behaviour unchanged, existing metrics and trace keys unchanged in name and meaning (`pipeline_ms`, `inference_ms`, `processing_fps`), each loss reason from the code path that decided it (never inferred from later frames), the canvas / toolbar boundaries verified, overhead measured (target < 0.1 ms per frame), bounded memory, replay equivalence, no "validated" before the user's hardware run.

**Stage 3 item 1, instrumentation (D55; built and tested offline 2026-10-26; hardware validation pending).** Observation only: nothing new feeds back into tracking, mapping, the engine, the controller or rendering; no condition changed.
- Where each reason comes from: `HandTracker.action` (track / track-miss / region-dropped / search-miss / search-hit / search-hit-unconfirmed); `HandPoints.status` (no-hand / tip-outside-view / mapping-failed / ok) and `raw` (fingertip in projector px before smoothing); the engine's own candidate before and after `update` (read, never written); `DrawingController.outcomes`, noted inside its existing branches: `added`, `duplicate` (`Stroke.append` skipped a repeat), `not_appended`, `refused_band` / `refused_panel` / `refused_no_point` (stroke start refused), `dropped_band` / `dropped_panel` / `dropped_no_point` (point filtered), `after_refused_start` (a point of a stroke refused at its start: the toolbar trap), `select`, `eraser`, `move`, `move_nothing`, `ignored_<tool>`. Kept apart from `log` (unchanged keys and counts).
- Frame state (`draw_diagnostics.frame_state`): start, lifted; in DRAW drawing / held (no usable hand, bridged) / gap (hand outside the keep band); otherwise start-unconfirmed (the engine discarded its buffered POINT frames in this frame), pending-start (POINT frames still being confirmed, also during a short held dropout), the hand status, or the mode.
- Geometry (`where`), from the code's own definitions: canvas [−0.5, w − 0.5) × [−0.5, h − 0.5) (`CoordinateMapper.inside_canvas`); toolbar `y ≥ 648` (`Toolbar.in_band`: no x and no lower limit, so a point below the canvas also counts as "band" for the controller; recorded as the controller decided, `where` says where it really was). Precedence: below, above, left, right (off the canvas, bottom/top first), then toolbar or canvas. Verified against `inside_canvas` and `in_band` on 5000 random points. Height bands as the baseline: top < 216, middle < 432, lower < 560, bottom < 648.
- Stage times per frame (ms): queue, inference, hand (shape + mapping + smoothing), engine, controller, ai, render, show (these sum to `pipeline_ms`), then diag, trace (writing the line), post (after `show` until the next frame); loop period. Fixed-bin histograms (bounded memory; percentiles to one bin, diag bins 0.005 ms).
- Trace: the old keys unchanged, plus action, status, tip_in_view, raw_proj, state, where, ink, stroke (attempt number), stroke_id, candidate, buffered, queue_ms, inference_ms, pipeline_ms, stage_ms. Trace file buffered (1 MB). Report: the old keys unchanged, plus `diagnostics` (tracker actions, hand status, states, ink outcomes by where, hand frames / losses / re-find per band, stroke attempts / committed / refused / attempts with points after a refusal, stage times, loop period, overhead with the 0.1 ms target); console summary lines.
- Files: `tools/draw_diagnostics.py` (new), `tools/draw.py`, `tools/tracking_session.py`, `interaction/drawing.py`; `tests/test_draw_diagnostics.py` (11 new), `tests/test_draw_tool.py` (4 new: toolbar trap; bottom strip with a hand loss and the lift; fingertip outside the view and below the canvas; timing and overhead; each also checks the old trace and report keys).
- Results (§13.2): suite before 547 passed / 22 skipped; after, run 1 561 passed / 22 skipped / 1 failed — a pre-existing intermittent test (R36), reproduced on the unchanged tree — and the final run 562 passed / 22 skipped. Replay equivalence on all 22 recorded draw sessions (196,969 frames): the original controller (git a0e2440) and the instrumented one gave identical strokes, logs, selections and tools. Overhead offline on the same frames: diag stage median 0.011 / p95 0.016 / p99 0.022 ms (target < 0.1: met offline); controller unchanged within noise (mean +0.0002 ms); trace line json 0.005 → 0.007 ms. Limitation: the old traces store the smoothed cursor, not the raw fingertip, so the replay feeds both controllers the same reconstructed inputs (engine events reproduced in 99.7 % of frames), not a camera replay.
- Replayed on the baseline recordings: B-3 trap run (`draw-20261026-161331`) refused_band 3, after_refused_start 1627, dropped_band 1123 points; B-3 repeated (`draw-20261026-161931`) dropped_band 269, after_refused_start 404, ignored_eraser 134.
- **Hardware validation (pending, the user):** `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --seconds 180 --trace --save-every 1`, charger connected, 3 minutes: free writing in the middle (touch); the toolbar trap (press a button by pointing, keep pointing and write upwards; then open the hand and write); a line just above the toolbar, dipping into it once; hand behind the back ~2 s mid-stroke; pointing below and left of the image; hover writing ~3 cm; hand down at the end. Checks: each event appears under its own reason; diag median < 0.1 ms live; fps and pipeline vs B-3 repeated (51.6 fps; with the hand 18.7 / 27.2 ms; inference 8.6 ms).
- **Hardware runs (2026-10-26, §13.2):** 17:20 (3 min) and 17:37 (1-min follow-up for the trap). Every report counter reconciles exactly with a recount of its trace; the saved canvases rebuild point for point from the traced `added` outcomes (an independent check: the canvas file is written by the model); the trap recorded as it happened (refused_band 1, 121 band points, 294 canvas points not drawn); live overhead diag median 0.045–0.05 ms, p95 0.070–0.075 ms (< 0.1; ~4× the offline 0.011 ms); 55.9 / 56.7 fps. **Confirmed on hardware:** added, refused_band, after_refused_start, dropped_band:toolbar, held, lifted, pending-start, start-unconfirmed, the band / loss / re-find counts, stage times. **Not exercised on hardware:** below-canvas rejection at the ink level (no stroke point was ever below the canvas: the hand was there only while hovering), tip-outside-view, mapping-failed, the panel, eraser / move / select outcomes. Limits found: "no-hand" cannot tell a missing hand from a missed detection (saved frames show the hand in view over ink, R34); stages with 0.05-ms bins only say "< 0.05"; the median of a small even count is the lower middle value; the baseline's "time to re-find" (B-3, 0.45 s) was a mean, the report gives median / p95 / max.

### 9.13 Smartboard mission (user, 2026-10-26): reliable drawing, the AI back, then a feature roadmap

**Goal and order (user):** (1) toolbar evidence review of the 17:38 run; (2) fix toolbar and stroke behaviour with the smallest safe change, regression tests, full suite, **real-hardware validation before calling it fixed**; (3) after (2) is tested and stable: investigate and restore the earlier AI feature (select → AI → answer in the right-side panel; local Ollama `qwen3-vl:8b-instruct`; no paid API; no mock answers; verify the model is really available; inference off the drawing path; graceful errors; never claim the AI understands handwriting beyond what the implemented vision path does); (4) test the integrated application, drawing performance measured with the AI idle and while it answers; (5) a prioritised feature roadmap, nothing built. Phase 2 constraints: toolbar stays at the bottom; tracking thresholds, smoothing, calibration, camera settings and the AI unchanged. No push without the user's approval.

**Phase 1 (2026-10-26), 17:38 run (§13.2):** the trap happened and was recorded: RED pressed by pointing; a stroke confirmed on RED and refused; the user kept pointing and wrote ~7 s: 121 points dropped in the band and 294 points on the canvas, none of them ink (the saved canvas rebuilds exactly from the accepted points); the only feedback was the plain cursor dot. Below-canvas rejection: **not exercised** (unverified on hardware). Also found: points left / right / above the canvas are drawn (R37). Over all 24 recorded sessions: 14 pen strokes in 7 sessions went into the toolbar band and came back and were joined straight across it (joins up to 512 px).

**Phase 2: D56 (built and tested offline 2026-10-26; hardware validation pending).** `interaction/drawing.py`: a live stroke whose point reaches the toolbar band (or the open AI panel) ends there exactly as a pen lift would (`_finish`: the pen stroke committed, an erase or a move one undo step, a SELECT loop selects); from then on, and after a refused start, `blocked` ("toolbar" / "panel") until the engine's real pen lift (STROKE_END): nothing is drawn and nothing is joined across the toolbar. `canvas/render.py`: while blocked and in DRAW the cursor is a grey ring with a slash (also with the D50 light, the dwell arc kept); over the canvas (not while on the toolbar) a hint at the top, left of an open panel: "Not drawing: open your hand, then point to draw" (kept away from the hand: projected text on the hand hinders tracking). Diagnostics (D55): outcomes `ended_band` / `ended_panel` and `after_ui_end`; state `blocked` (after `held`: the hand first); report `strokes.ended_on_ui`, `points_after_ui_end`; trace `blocked`; log `strokes_ended_on_toolbar` / `_panel`. Engine, tracking, gestures, smoothing, the pen-lift delay, calibration, camera and the AI unchanged. Not changed, for the user to decide: the D49 bridging of short interruptions that contain an open hand (open question 18); off-canvas ink left / right / above (R37).
- Evidence: replay of the 24 sessions (210,434 frames, the same engine events into the old and the new controller, each session's own toolbar layout): 354 committed strokes in both; 17 sessions identical; in 7 sessions 10 strokes differ, each one that reached the toolbar and came back (now ended at the band); 29 band ends in all. Render cost while blocked +0.46 ms median (0.86 → 1.32 ms; only while blocked).
- **Hardware validation protocol (fixed 2026-10-26 before the run):** charger connected; `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --seconds 120 --trace --save-every 0.5`. (1) Write a word in the middle. (2) Point at a colour button until it is pressed, keep pointing and write upward: nothing is drawn, the slashed ring and the top hint show; open the hand ~1 s (the ring becomes normal), point and write: ink. (3) Write a line just above the toolbar, dip into the toolbar mid-line and come back up still pointing: the line ends at the toolbar edge, the slashed ring shows, nothing joins; open the hand, point, finish the line. (4) While pointing, move down through the toolbar and below the projected image, then back up. (5) Free writing to the end. **Pass:** (a) ≥ 1 stroke ended at the toolbar (`ended_on_ui`), and in the trace no `added` point follows an `ended_band` in the same attempt; (b) the trap: `refused_band` ≥ 1, its canvas points `after_refused_start`, none in the saved canvas (rebuild check); (c) blocked frames show the slashed ring and the hint in the saved camera frames, and the user saw them on the wall; (d) after each lift the next stroke draws; (e) no stroke point in the saved canvas at y ≥ 648; (f) processing fps and pipeline not lower than the 17:38 run (56.7 fps; median 16.8 / p95 28.3 ms) beyond its spread. Step (4) also records whether below-canvas rejection is reachable at all (it needs a stroke point emitted below the canvas, i.e. the hand tracked there while pointing).
- **D56 hardware run 18:06 (§13.2): partly validated.** (b) trap: 2 refusals, each blocked until the lift ✓; (d) the next stroke drew after each lift ✓; (e) no canvas point at y ≥ 648 ✓; (f) 56.7 fps, pipeline 16.4 / 28.3 ms ✓; **(a) not exercised** (no stroke ran from the canvas into the toolbar: `ended_on_ui` 0); **(c) not confirmed** (the blocked frames were on the toolbar, where the slashed ring sits under the fingertip and the camera does not show it; the hint was up for 21 frames; the user's view not reported).

**Phase 3 (2026-10-26): the AI, inspected and run for real (no hardware run yet).**
- *Architecture (all present, connected and unchanged since D54; nothing missing or broken found):* activation `draw --ai` or `[ai] enabled` (off by default, so the recent hardware runs had no AI button: report `"ai": null`); config `[ai]` (`config.AIConfig`: backend local, `qwen3-vl:8b-instruct`, `http://127.0.0.1:11434`, timeout 120 s); selection `canvas/selection.py` (`select_in_loop` with the D53 loop closing; nothing selected = the whole canvas); model input `selection_image` + `png_bytes` (the selected **strokes**, stored as point lists, rendered in their own colours on white, cropped, longer side ≤ 1024 px, PNG); inference `ai/local.py` `LocalAssistant` (Ollama `/api/chat`: system prompt, task, the PNG, JSON-schema answer {transcription, result} (+ readable, topic for EXPLORE), temperature 0, token caps 1024 / 400) run by `ai/worker.py` `AIWorker` (one daemon thread, one request at a time, `submit` / `poll`, PNG + JSON saved to `data/ai/`); actions `ai/client.py` `ACTIONS` (the six instructions) and `canvas/panel.py` `PANEL_ACTIONS`; the controller `interaction/drawing.py` (AI button, panel states menu / thinking / answer / error, `ai_jobs`, busy refusal, "Nothing written to read yet."); the panel `canvas/render.py` `_panel` (READ line, action header, EXPLORE topic, wrap, "..." when out of room). **No OCR and no stored text:** the vision-language model reads the handwriting from the image and answers in the same call; the transcription is shown (READ) and saved with the request, not kept on the canvas. Reading quality = that model's: S2 PASS on the user's judgment (§13.2).
- *Environment (checked 2026-10-26 18:1x):* Ollama 0.40.11 installed (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Ollama`) and running, API answering; `qwen3-vl:8b-instruct` (70e58e37beb1, 8.2B, Q4_K_M, vision) present locally (also the unused thinking variant `qwen3-vl:8b`); loaded **100 % on the GPU**, context 4096. Nothing downloaded.
- *Real inference through the application's own code (controller → SELECT loop → AI button → panel action → `AIWorker` + `LocalAssistant` → Ollama → `ai_answer` → rendered panel; no projector or camera; §13.2):* all six actions answered by the real model, each read "KRITAGYA IS MY FRIEND" correctly from the user's finger writing (the red "Ved" left out by the loop), 0.59–1.93 s after a 6.1-s warm-up; EXPLORE topic "Friendship"; EXPLORE on the 2026-10-25 "WHAT IS SQL" image: topic SQL, 2.38 s. Busy refusal, empty canvas, missing model (HTTP 404), Ollama unreachable (~2 s) and a timeout each give their panel message; nothing raised. Not seen: an answer long enough to fill the panel (the answers were 29–82 tokens); the "..." cut and the token-cap message are covered by unit tests only.
- *Concurrency:* inference runs in Ollama's own process on the GPU, reached from a daemon thread that mostly waits on the socket; the frame loop only polls. In the frame loop on the press: the selection image + PNG (14.4 ms once per request on this canvas: one long frame). Offline proxy (234 saved camera frames through the real tracker + renderer, 702 frames per phase): tracking median 8.01 ms idle → 8.50 ms during back-to-back real requests (p95 18.3 → 19.5), tracking + render 9.92 → 10.43 ms, frames over 16.7 ms 18.8 % in both; Ollama used ~4 % of one CPU core. **The earlier live slowdowns while answering (S2 / EXPLORE runs: 23.6–32.5 fps vs ~40) are not reproduced by the proxy**; not in it: the camera thread, the operator window and the display path. The NVIDIA GPU that runs the model also drives the projector (HDMI; `nvidia-smi` display_active Enabled; the Intel GPU drives the laptop panel): R13 confirmed; hypothesis (unproven): presenting frames competes with inference on that GPU. The live run's stage times decide it (trace `ai_busy` added: per frame, whether a request was running).
- *Change:* `tools/draw.py` trace `ai_busy` (observation only; test extended). No AI, worker, controller or panel change: the implementation works.
- **Hardware validation protocol (fixed 2026-10-26 before the run; criterion S3 unchanged: while a request runs, processing ≥ 30 fps and drawing still works):** charger connected, Ollama running; `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --ai --seconds 300 --trace --save-every 1`. (1) ~60 s: write a short topic in the left two-thirds (e.g. WHAT IS GRAVITY) and something else; no AI request (AI enabled, idle). (2) SELECT, loop around the topic, PEN, AI button: the panel opens. (3) Press SUMMARIZE, EXPLAIN, SIMPLIFY, EXAMPLES, QUESTIONS, EXPLORE in turn; while each one is "reading", draw a few strokes on the left. (4) CLOSE, hold CLEAR 2 s, AI, SUMMARIZE: "Nothing written to read yet." (5) Draw normally to the end. Judged: six real answers in the panel, each READ line correct for the selection (the user's judgment for the answers); S3 on the frames with `ai_busy`; fps / pipeline and per-stage times idle vs busy vs the AI-off run 18:06 (56.7 fps, 16.4 / 28.3 ms); frame gaps > 25 ms; no crash; toolbar and D56 behaviour as before.

**Full-project hardware test (fixed 2026-10-27 before the run; on battery allowed, §2.1; the power state recorded):** one session with the shipped config (AI on by default, D57): `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --seconds 420 --trace --save-every 1`, then `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --seconds 30 --no-ai`. Steps: (A) start without a flag: setup check passes or recovers, the AI button is on the toolbar; (B) PEN, write a word in the middle; (C) press RED and THICK with an open hand, write a second word; (D) with PEN draw a line down into the toolbar, keep pointing and come back up, then open the hand ~1 s; (E) point at BLUE until it is pressed, keep pointing and write upward, open the hand, point and write; (F) ERASER, erase part of one word, PEN; (G) MOVE, drag one word, PEN; (H) UNDO twice, REDO once; (I) write a short topic on the left half, SELECT, loop around it, PEN, AI, then the six actions one by one, drawing a few strokes on the left while each is "reading"; (J) CLOSE, hold CLEAR 2 s, AI, SUMMARIZE ("Nothing written to read yet."), CLOSE; (K) free drawing to the end. **Pass:** (1) no crash, report written; (2) the strokes, the red thick word (colour and width in the canvas JSON); (3) D56: ≥ 1 PEN stroke `ended_band` with no `added` point after it in that attempt, the refused start drew nothing, no canvas point at y ≥ 648, the slashed ring and hint seen by the user; (4) `erase_strokes` ≥ 1, `moves` ≥ 1, undo 2 / redo 1; (5) AI: the six actions answered (ok), READ lines judged by the user, ≥ 1 stroke with `added` points on `ai_busy` frames (drawing during inference), ≥ 30 fps on `ai_busy` frames (S3), the empty-canvas message; (6) speed reported (fps, pipeline, stages split by `ai_busy`) against the 18:42 / 18:46 runs; (7) `--no-ai`: report `ai` null, no AI button.

### 9.14 Pages, documents and the AI on documents (user request 2026-10-27; D59, D60, D61)

**Request (user, 2026-10-27), in this order:** (A) a multi-page whiteboard: add page, previous, next, a page indicator ("Page 2 of 5"), limits greyed out or stated, every page its own content (a new page blank), drawing / erasing / selection / AI on the current page, undo / redo never across pages, no interference with tracking or calibration, saved if practical; (B) open PNG / JPG / PDF from a native Windows file picker, fitted with the aspect ratio kept, PDF page turning with an indicator, a clear way back to the whiteboard with no data destroyed, the file never changed, optional annotations kept separate, clear error messages; whiteboard pages, reference documents and annotations are separate concepts; (C) the existing AI panel on the shown image or rendered PDF page (SUMMARIZE, EXPLAIN, SIMPLIFY, EXAMPLES, QUESTIONS, EXPLORE, and answering the user's question) with the real page image as the model's input, the handwriting AI preserved, the panel saying what is analysed, a question input that does not disturb the gestures, graceful failures, readable answers, local AI only; (D) automated tests and the full suite with exact counts, physical tests prepared separately; (E) brain.md, README, focused commits, no push. Constraints (user): hand-tracking thresholds, gesture classification, the pen-lift delay, coordinate mapping and selection behaviour unchanged unless a specific necessary change is explained; no paid service or cloud AI; stop and ask on a major conflict; never claim physical success the user has not seen.

**User decisions (2026-10-27, before building):** a PAGES button on the toolbar opening a compact panel; QUESTIONS kept and ASK added, the question typed on the laptop keyboard and shown live on the wall (Enter sends, Esc cancels); `pypdfium2` for PDFs; D57 and D58 committed first (done: d7de8b7, e687287).

**Design.** Three separate objects. The **board** (`canvas/board.py`): pages, each a `Canvas` with its own `History`. The **reference document** (`canvas/reference.py`): the file (read once, never written), its current page, the fitted page for the wall (cached for the shown page only). Its **annotations**: one `Canvas` + `History` per document page, inside the reference, never mixed into the file or a whiteboard page. The controller draws on one *layer* at a time (`_layer()`: the shown document page's annotations in view "doc", else the current whiteboard page); `canvas` and `history` are properties of that layer, so the pen, eraser, MOVE, SELECT, UNDO / REDO, CLEAR and the AI act on what is shown with no change of their own. Before the layer changes (page turn, view switch, document opened or closed) whatever was live ends on its own layer (`_finish`) and the selection is dropped; a pointing stroke still going on draws nothing on the new layer until the pen lifts (D56's `blocked`). Closing a document, or opening another, keeps its annotations by file (given back when the file is opened again, and saved with the board). The AI on a document reads the page itself (`Reference.ai_png`: the picture, or the PDF page rendered by PDFium at 1600 px on the longer side), not the annotations; its request has a context (`{"source": "document", "page": ...}`; ASK adds `"question"`), and the system prompt and tasks speak of a page (`SYSTEM_DOCUMENT`, `DOCUMENT_ACTIONS`); the answer format, model, worker and token caps are those of D51 / D52 / D54.

**Changes outside the new features, each needed and kept small:**
- Toolbar: one more button (PAGES) in the last group: the buttons narrow from 70 to 65 px with the AI button (77 → 70 px without); the longest label (ERASER, 61 px) still fits; dwell times unchanged.
- AI panel: ASK takes the free cell left of CLOSE in the last row, so the answer area keeps its 374 px (D51).
- `draw` now reads the key returned by its existing `cv2.pollKey()` call (before, it was discarded, so a key pressed in that part of a frame was lost); needed so no letter of a typed question is lost; the shortcut keys gain the same way. While a question is typed every key is text (Esc cancels the question, not the session). No gesture path is involved.
- Documents are fitted with ≥ 12 px of white around them (`MARGIN`) so D58's setup watch still finds the canvas outline over a dark page.

Not changed: hand tracking and its thresholds, gesture classification, the engine (pen-lift delay, D49), coordinate mapping, calibration, the camera, selection, the AI backend / model / worker, and every existing AI request on handwriting (byte-identical, tested).

**Automated acceptance criteria (fixed 2026-10-27 before the tests were written) — all met in software, 2026-10-27:**
- A1 add / previous / next and "Page k of n"; at the first and last page the arrow is greyed out and does nothing (`page_limit`). A2 every page keeps its own strokes; a new page is blank. A3 pen, eraser, CLEAR, SELECT and the AI act on the current page; UNDO / REDO never reach another page. A4 a page change in the middle of a stroke draws nothing on the new page. A5 every page saved (board file + a PNG per page) and resumed (`--resume`, `--load`). A6 PAGES and + PAGE by dwell in `draw`. (`tests/test_board.py`, `tests/test_draw_tool.py`)
- B1 PNG / JPG (also a non-ASCII path) fitted with the aspect ratio kept, a white margin, nothing under the toolbar. B2 PDF pages turned with "PDF page k of n"; each page rendered. B3 unsupported, damaged and missing files give a short message on the wall; drawing goes on. B4 annotations on their own layer per document page with their own undo; the file's SHA-256 unchanged. B5 SHOW BOARD / SHOW DOC keep both as left; CLOSE DOC returns to the whiteboard page as it was. B6 OPEN FILE by dwell in `draw` (dialog faked); a cancelled dialog changes nothing. B7 the board file keeps the open document (path, page, annotations) and closed documents' annotations; a missing document gives a message and the board still loads. B8 closing or replacing a document keeps its annotations; reopening the file gives them back. B9 the setup watch still sees the canvas over a near-black page. (`tests/test_reference.py`, `tests/test_draw_tool.py`)
- C1 the model's input is the page itself: the PNG sent decodes to exactly the rendered page (no annotations), also end to end through `draw`. C2 documents get the page prompt and tasks; handwriting requests unchanged. C3 ASK: typed text shown live, Backspace, a 160-character limit, non-text keys ignored, Enter on an empty question does nothing, Esc returns to the menu with nothing sent. C4 while a question is typed, strokes and panel buttons work, a late answer does not replace the question, and in `draw` `z` / `s` / `c` / `o` are text and Esc does not end the session. C5 the panel title says what is read ("AI on PDF page 2 of 3", "AI on the image", "AI on the selection", "AI on the whole canvas"), also for an answer to a request made before a document was opened; "PAGE:" instead of "READ:"; at most 2 lines of it. C6 a page that cannot be prepared, and a missing question, give a message; nothing raises. C7 each answer keeps its context in the report and in `data/ai/`. (`tests/test_document_ai.py`, `tests/test_draw_tool.py`)

**Real local model on a PDF, through the application's own code (2026-10-27; no projector, camera or hand; §13.2):** a 2-page A4 lesson PDF with text (made for the test) opened by `Reference`, the AI panel driven through the controller (ASK typed with `type_key`), `LocalAssistant` + Ollama `qwen3-vl:8b-instruct`: 9 of 9 answers ok and correct for the page (my check against the page text; the user's judgment is part of the physical test); the image sent decoded to exactly the rendered page in all 9; prompt 1,911–2,046 tokens (context 4,096), answers 65–137 tokens, 1.7–4.6 s each (three runs: 1.7–5.6 s); rendering the page for the AI 3–5 ms + PNG 10–22 ms on the press; fitting a page for the wall 8–33 ms once per page. ASK "Who invented the telephone?" on the photosynthesis page: "It does not mention the telephone or its inventor. Answer not on page." (no guess). In 5 of 9 answers the model copied the whole page into the transcription despite the prompt (ASK ×3, QUESTIONS, EXAMPLES): the panel shows at most 2 lines of it (the answer stayed in view in all 9 rendered panels). A clearer JSON-schema field description was tried and changed nothing (the same 5 copies; Ollama does not put field descriptions in the prompt: identical prompt token counts) and was reverted.

**Physical acceptance test (protocol fixed 2026-10-27 before any run; runs 1–3 partial, §13.2).** Before: Ollama running; one room light on; battery allowed (§2.1), the power state recorded; on the laptop a PDF of ≥ 3 pages with text (≥ 12 pt) and a JPG (a photo or a diagram); note the PDF's SHA-256 (`certutil -hashfile file.pdf SHA256`). Command: `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --seconds 600 --trace --save-every 1`.
- P1 Pages: write ONE in the middle; PAGES → `+ PAGE`: blank, "Page 2 of 2"; write TWO; `< PAGE`: ONE is back; `< PAGE` again: greyed, nothing happens; `PAGE >`: TWO; UNDO on page 2 removes only TWO's last stroke; `< PAGE`: page 1 unchanged; `PAGE >`, REDO.
- P2 With the board panel open, draw a line from the middle into the panel while pointing: it ends at the panel's edge (slashed ring); open the hand, point, draw again.
- P3 `OPEN FILE` (dwell): the Windows Open dialog appears on the laptop; choose the PDF: page 1 fitted on the wall, "PDF page 1 of n - name" at the top left; `DOC >` to the last page (`DOC >` greyed there), `< DOC` back to page 2.
- P4 RED, circle a word on page 2; `DOC >`: page 3 is clean; `< DOC`: the circle is there; UNDO removes the circle only; REDO.
- P5 `SHOW BOARD`: the whiteboard page as left; `SHOW DOC`: PDF page 2 as left; `CLOSE DOC`: the whiteboard; `o` key, the same PDF: the circle is back on page 2.
- P6 `o` key: choose a file that is not a real PDF/image (e.g. a .txt renamed .pdf): a message on the wall, drawing goes on; `o` again and cancel the dialog: nothing changes.
- P7 AI on the PDF (a text page): AI → the title reads "AI on PDF page k of n"; SUMMARIZE, EXPLAIN (draw a few strokes on the left while it is "reading"), QUESTIONS, EXPLORE.
- P8 ASK: type a question about the page on the laptop keyboard (it appears on the wall as typed), one Backspace, Enter; then ASK, type a few letters including `z` and `s`, Esc (back to the menu, the session goes on); ASK a question the page does not answer.
- P9 `o` key, the JPG: AI → "AI on the image"; EXPLAIN.
- P10 `SHOW BOARD`, SELECT a loop around writing, AI → "AI on the selection", SUMMARIZE (the handwriting AI as before).
- P11 ESC; then `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --resume --seconds 60`: the pages, the document and its annotations come back.

**Pass:** (1) no crash; the report written; (2) `board.pages` ≥ 2 with the strokes per page as drawn, `page:add` ≥ 1, `page_limit` ≥ 1; (3) the PDF fitted without distortion and the indicator right (the user's view and the saved frames); annotations only on their page (`reference.annotated_pages`); the PDF's SHA-256 unchanged; (4) SHOW BOARD / CLOSE DOC left the whiteboard as it was; the circle came back on reopening (`doc:annotations_back` 1); (5) the bad file's message seen, `open_failed` 1, `open_cancelled` ≥ 1; (6) every AI answer ok, `context.source` "document" in P7–P9 and "selection" in P10, the answers right for the page (the user's judgment), the title right each time; the question shown live and with its answer; `ask_cancelled` 1 and the session not ended by that Esc; (7) during a document request ≥ 1 stroke with `added` points on `ai_busy` frames and ≥ 30 fps on those frames (S3); (8) resume restored the pages, the document and its annotations. Recorded, not gated: hand finding and fps in view "doc" vs "board" (trace `view`), setup-watch reports with documents shown (0 expected without a move), whether the keyboard reached the app right after the Open dialog closed (L10).

**Limitations (known, accepted for now):**
- L1 The AI panel (440 px) and the board panel (300 px) cover the right part of the wall while open; a document page under them is hidden there (a portrait A4 page loses its right ~40 px under the AI panel; a wide image much more). Close the panel to see the whole page. Not moved aside: the annotations are tied to the page's position.
- L2 A document puts projected content on the hand; a busy or dark page may lower hand finding as heavy ink did (R34, G8: 44 % in a dark room, 96 % with a room light). To be measured in the physical run (trace `view`).
- L3 The AI reads the whole shown page, not the annotations and not a selected part of it; small print on a large page (a poster) rendered at 1600 px may be unreadable to the model.
- L4 Answers are short (≈ 80 words, the 374-px panel; longer ones end with "..."); the page line shows ≤ 2 lines.
- L5 EXPLORE's and ASK's facts come from the 8B model's memory (R35); ASK may say the page does not answer a question instead of answering from general knowledge (seen: the telephone question).
- L6 Fitting a PDF page for the wall takes 8–33 ms once per page turn, and preparing it for the AI 13–27 ms on the press: one long frame each.
- L7 The document is saved by path, not copied; if it is moved or deleted, `--resume` restores the board and says the document was not reopened. Annotations of a page that no longer exists (the file changed) are dropped.
- L8 One document at a time (opening another closes the first, its annotations kept).
- L9 The Open dialog blocks the frame loop while it is open (the pen is lifted first, the frames meanwhile are skipped).
- L10 Typed keys reach the app only while one of its windows (projector or operator) has the keyboard focus; after the Open dialog closes, Windows normally gives the focus back, to be confirmed in the physical run.

### 9.15 Smart Lines (user request 2026-10-27; D62, D63, D64, D65)

**Request (user, 2026-10-27):** an additional LINES tool next to PEN (PEN unchanged; freehand ink never turned into text): a comfortable fixed writing zone, an explicit COMPLETE LINE, completed lines placed from the top and numbered, configurable look, the original handwriting always kept (a failed or wrong recognition never destroys it), an unfinished line never silently dropped when switching; recognition local, asynchronous, never blocking drawing, with a correction / confirmation workflow and never a silent rewrite by an LLM; an Edit Page (edit, delete, move lines, renumbering, stable ids, undoable); the AI assistant available in both modes. Rules: no change to calibration, tracking, landmarks, mapping or hardware settings; no new package or model downloaded; phase by phase, stop after each phase for review; no commit or push without approval.

**Phase 0, recognition benchmark (2026-10-27, approved by the user; no repository change, scratch files only; §13.2).** Windows Ink (the built-in WinRT `InkRecognizerContainer`, English (US) recognizer, reached through PowerShell with no download) against `qwen3-vl:8b-instruct` in a transcription-only prompt (no correction, completion or paraphrase; `[?]` for an unreadable character; temperature 0), on the same strokes: Windows Ink the stored points, Qwen an image of them from the app's own `selection_image`. Labelled finger writing recorded with `draw` (protocol fixed before recording; recording 1 off by one page and not scored; recording 2 labelled by the line content, labels frozen before any recognizer ran, user confirmation pending). **All 22 distinct lines: CER 16.1 % vs 1.0 %, WER 48.0 % vs 6.0 %, exact 11/22 vs 20/22; paired: both right 11, only Qwen 9, only Windows 0, neither 2 (exact McNemar p = 0.004, one writer).** Windows Ink: 11–65 ms per line plus 0.6–1.0 s per fresh PowerShell call; Qwen 1.31–1.55 s (warm, without tracking load). Qwen also completed illegible glyphs (a blob read as the E of CYCLE) and invented one word for an overwritten one ("GRATED" for GOOD); it kept misspellings as written (KRITGYA, MORNIG). Agreement of the two predicted Qwen's correctness in 11 of 11 lines; too few lines to rely on.

**User decisions (2026-10-27, after Phase 0):** the recognizer is the existing `qwen3-vl:8b-instruct` (D63); LINES is an additional toolbar tool, all others unchanged; the AI assistant available in both modes; no packages or models installed; typeset text through Pillow with installed Windows fonts, a fixed writing zone and a scrolling notes area, Qwen as a second opinion deferred — design notes for later phases; implementation in phases: (1) foundation, (2) Qwen recognition with teacher confirmation, (3) tests; stop after Phase 1 for review.

**Phase 1 design (D62, built 2026-10-27).**
- `canvas/lines.py` (new, pure): `Line` (stable id + the strokes as written: same `Stroke` objects, ids, points, colours, widths; never resampled), `LineSheet` (a page's Smart Lines: the completed lines in order, the unfinished line as its own `Canvas`, one `History` for both, the notes' scroll, a version for the render cache; `to_dict` / `from_dict`, format `invisible-canvas/smart-lines` v1), `CompleteLine` (an undo command: the unfinished strokes become the next line; the same `Line` and id return on redo), `LinesLayout` (the writing zone, the notes area, COMPLETE LINE right of the zone, SCROLL UP / DOWN right of the notes area; checked inside the canvas, above the toolbar, not overlapping), `render_sheet` (notes area: each line's own handwriting scaled to the line height after its number; "k-m of n" when scrolled; the zone with the unfinished line; cached per change).
- `canvas/board.py`: `Page.lines` (made when first used); the board file keeps them under the optional key `smart_lines` (one entry per page or null), written only when some page has Smart Lines, so unused it is unchanged and older files load.
- `interaction/drawing.py`: the tools act on `target` — the shown page as before, or with LINES the page's line sheet. LINES: a pointing stroke must start inside the zone (else refused, `blocked` "zone", hint "open your hand, then point inside the line box"); the stroke is the pen's (colour and width as selected; a colour press keeps LINES); UNDO / REDO / CLEAR act on the Smart Lines only (CLEAR: the unfinished line); COMPLETE LINE / SCROLL by dwell (1 s); a stroke that reaches a Smart Lines button ends there (D56). Switching the tool into or out of LINES first finishes what is live on the old target. LINES works on whiteboard pages; a shown document pauses it (back to PEN, with a notice); pressing LINES on a document gives a notice. An open panel now covers what lies under it for dwell presses (the AI panel lies over COMPLETE LINE). The AI reads the page's freehand ink / selection as before (Smart Lines are not sent in Phase 1).
- `canvas/render.py`: Smart Lines come with `controller.background` (as a document page does, D60); with LINES the buttons and their dwell progress are drawn (grey when they cannot act); the zone hint; toolbar labels that would touch the edges drawn slightly smaller.
- `canvas/toolbar.py`: LINES in the tool group (17 buttons with the AI button, 61 px wide; 65 → 61).
- `config.py` / `config/default.toml`: `[smart_lines]` zone [24, 360, 1080, 600] (the middle height: the bottom band loses the hand most, §9.12), notes [24, 40, 900, 336] (below the page label, left of the PAGES panel; 6 lines of 40 px + 8 px), numbering "1." ("1)", "(1)", "none").
- `tools/draw.py`: the layout from the config (a wrong one stops with a message); report `smart_lines` (per page: lines, ids, strokes per line, unfinished strokes); trace `tool`; the per-page PNG shows the notes.
- Not changed: tracking, landmarks, gestures, the engine, calibration, mapping, camera, the AI backend and its prompts, the other tools' behaviour.

**Automated acceptance criteria for Phase 1 (fixed 2026-10-27 before the tests were written):** SL1 the layout fits the canvas above the toolbar, left of / below the PAGES panel, equals the config; a wrong config is refused. SL2 LINES writes only inside the zone (a start outside is refused and stays blocked until the lift); PEN draws anywhere as before. SL3 COMPLETE LINE moves exactly the written strokes (same objects, ids, points, colours, widths) into line 1, 2, ...; with nothing written it gives a message and makes no line. SL4 UNDO / REDO with LINES cover the unfinished strokes and COMPLETE LINE (the same line and id come back), never the freehand page; with PEN never the Smart Lines. SL5 CLEAR with LINES clears only the unfinished line (undoable). SL6 the unfinished line survives PEN, ERASER, page changes and a document (LINES pauses and is refused there); a line stroke still going on when PEN is pressed ends in the line and draws nothing more. SL7 a colour keeps LINES. SL8 the notes area scrolls to the newest line, SCROLL UP / DOWN by dwell, limits greyed, still in view after an undo. SL9 rendering: without Smart Lines the background is None (as before); the zone, buttons, faint zone with PEN, the zone hint; cached until a change. SL10 a line stroke reaching COMPLETE LINE ends there. SL11 the board file keeps lines, ids, strokes, the unfinished line and the scroll; ids continue after loading; unused, the file is unchanged; older files load. SL12 the AI stays available with LINES and reads the page's ink; COMPLETE LINE under the open AI panel cannot be pressed. SL13 end to end in `draw`: LINES and COMPLETE LINE by dwell, report, board file, `--resume`. (`tests/test_smart_lines.py`, `tests/test_draw_tool.py`, `tests/test_canvas.py`)

**Physical test for Phase 1 (protocol fixed 2026-10-27 before any run; run 1 partial: LP1, LP8 pass, §13.2).** `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --seconds 600 --trace --save-every 1`. LP1 LINES; write a word in the zone; COMPLETE LINE: it appears as line 1 in the notes area, the zone is empty. LP2 write 7 more lines (completing each): the notes area shows 6 and scrolls; SCROLL UP / DOWN. LP3 start a stroke above the zone with LINES: nothing drawn, the zone hint; open the hand, point inside the zone: ink. LP4 write half a line, PEN, draw freehand, PAGES → + PAGE and back, LINES: the half line is still there; COMPLETE LINE. LP5 UNDO twice with LINES (a stroke, then the last COMPLETE LINE: the line goes back into the zone), REDO. LP6 RED with LINES: still LINES, red ink. LP7 AI → SUMMARIZE with LINES: the panel works; drawing goes on. LP8 ESC, then `draw --resume --seconds 60`: lines, numbers and the unfinished line are back. **Pass:** each step as described on the wall (the user's view and the saved frames); report `smart_lines` and actions matching; no freehand ink created by LINES; fps not lower than the recent runs (43–56 fps) beyond their spread.

**Limitations (Phase 1):** (1) no recognition and no Edit Page yet (Phases 2–3); lines can only be removed by UNDO of their COMPLETE LINE. (2) the AI panel and the board panel lie over parts of the zone / COMPLETE LINE while open. (3) a line wider than the notes area is shrunk to fit (long lines get small). (4) the eraser, MOVE and SELECT act on freehand ink only; the unfinished line is corrected with UNDO / CLEAR. (5) Smart Lines are whiteboard-only (not on documents). (6) toolbar buttons 61 px wide (about 6 cm on the wall). (7) strokes outside the zone are refused at their start only; a stroke started in the zone may leave it (kept as written).

**Phase 2: the interface and reading with the teacher's confirmation (D64; proposal with mockups approved by the user 2026-10-27, built the same day).** User decisions: (1) the clean design: subtle corner guides, a faint baseline, a compact DONE, readable notes, slim scrolling controls; (2) Pillow used directly for Segoe UI (already installed, nothing downloaded); (3) `qwen3-vl:8b-instruct` with two texts: the literal transcription and a cleaned text with contextual spelling correction; (4) OK, AS WRITTEN, EDIT, KEEP INK, RETRY, nothing accepted automatically; (5) the original strokes and the teacher's edits kept; a stale reading ignored after an edit, a confirmation or a retry; (6) recognition asynchronous, the AI assistant in both modes; (7) the two-text prompt validated on the 22 labelled lines, literal and corrected reported separately, the corrected text never counted for the literal score; (8) automated tests (recognition, review actions, retries, failures, undo / redo, late responses, saving / resuming, regressions); (9) a replay test of tracking during recognition, with its limitations; (10) brain.md and the full suite. Not to change: tracking, landmarks, calibration, mapping; no install, download, commit or push. Order: interface → recognition → validation; stop before the next wall test.
- `canvas/text.py` (new): Windows TrueType fonts through Pillow (any Unicode: é, ×, °), `fit` / `fit_end` (bisection), `draw` (each text, rounded box or triangle goes through Pillow on its own small region: a whole 1280 × 720 frame there and back costs ~7 ms).
- `canvas/lines.py`: `Line.reading` (set by the recognizer only) and `Line.decision` (the teacher's: ok / as_written / edit / ink, with its text), kept apart; `status` follows from them (recognizing, check, failed, confirmed, ink); `request` (the reading awaited; not saved). `ReviewLine` and `RetryLine` are commands in the page's Smart Lines history (a decision drops the request awaited; RETRY sets the reading and decision aside, undo brings both back). Format v2 (v1 still loads; its lines are then read). Layout (`LinesLayout`; geometry superseding Phase 1's): zone [40, 400, 1040, 590] with four corner guides, a baseline at 76 % of its height and "Write here"; DONE 120 × 56 px right of it; notes [40, 40, 900, 336] (6 rows of 40 + 6 px, each row pickable by dwell); a 6-px scroll track with a thumb and 40 × 40 arrows right of the notes; the review bar (40, 340)–(1240, 392) between notes and zone with OK / AS WRITTEN / EDIT / KEEP INK / RETRY (40 px tall) on its right; the zone must lie ≥ 60 px below the notes. Notes by status: confirmed text dark (Segoe UI 30 px), a suggestion blue with a dot, the handwriting grey with "reading..." or an orange "not recognised - RETRY or EDIT", kept ink in its own colours, numbers grey, "k-m of n". Drawing in layers: the notes (each line's ink drawn small once and kept on the line), the zone (a new stroke draws only itself), laid together darkest-wins; the controls (`render_controls`) typeset when what they show changes and copied over each frame.
- `ai/recognize.py` (new): the system prompt's `written` instructions are Phase 0's literal-only prompt word for word, followed by the `text` instructions (correct clear misspellings and missing spaces from the context; keep every word, number, symbol, order and case; never add, remove, translate, rephrase or complete; keep [?]); schema {written, text}; temperature 0; 256 tokens. `reading_from_response`: spaces normalised; a `written` with no letter or digit → not recognised whatever `text` says (no suggestion from nothing); an empty `text` → `written`; cut off, no text, an unreadable answer → not recognised. `LineRecognizer` (Ollama `/api/chat` through the existing local client; every failure a reading with a message, nothing raises). `RecognitionWorker`: a thread reading queued lines one after another; `cancel` skips one not started; each image and reading saved to `data/ai/lines/`.
- `interaction/drawing.py`: `take_line_jobs` (every line on any page with neither a reading nor a decision and no request awaited: new lines, retries, undone decisions, lines from a file; without a reader marked not recognised), `take_line_cancels`, `line_result` (used only if the line still awaits exactly that request; a line undone meanwhile keeps it for the redo). `review_line`: the line being edited, else the one picked in the notes, else the first waiting (check / failed), else the newest being read; the bar only with LINES. Review buttons and note rows by dwell (1 s); greyed when they cannot act (AS WRITTEN when the literal text is the suggestion; RETRY without a reader or while reading). EDIT is typed on the laptop as ASK (Enter saves, Esc cancels, Backspace; ≤ 120 characters; ends when LINES, the page or the view is left, or on UNDO / REDO / CLEAR); `typing` = ASK or EDIT, `asking` = ASK; one at a time. A line stroke reaching the review bar ends there (D56).
- `canvas/render.py`: the controls' patches and the hold-to-press progress. `tools/draw.py`: the reader (only with the AI on; always the local model, free, whatever `[ai] backend`), the hand-off in the frame loop, trace `lines_busy`, report `smart_lines` (status counts, each line's decision, text and reading) and `line_recognition` (requests, read, not read, late readings ignored, latency, fps and tracker time while reading). `config.py` / `config/default.toml`: the geometry above, `font = "segoeui.ttf"`, `text_size = 30`. `pyproject.toml`: pillow.
- Not changed: tracking, landmarks, gestures, the engine, calibration, mapping, camera, the AI panel's backend and prompts.

**Automated checks for Phase 2 (the user's item 8; `tests/test_smart_lines_review.py`, 22 tests, plus the updated Phase 1 tests and `tests/test_draw_tool.py`):** SL14 one request asks for both texts (model, schema, temperature 0, token cap, the image). SL15 readings and every unusable answer (cut off, no text, not JSON, nothing readable, a correction from nothing) and every transport failure (Ollama not running, timeout, model missing, other) become readings with a message. SL16 the worker reads in order, skips a cancelled request, saves image and reading. SL17 DONE shows the line at once and queues one request; a reading becomes a suggestion, not accepted. SL18 OK / AS WRITTEN / KEEP INK (and OK over kept ink) are undoable; undoing DONE and redoing brings the reading back; PEN undo never touches them. SL19 AS WRITTEN greyed when the literal text is the suggestion. SL20 EDIT on the keyboard (z / y / s are text), Enter / Esc / empty Enter, review buttons not pressable while typing, leaving LINES ends it. SL21 EDIT and ASK take turns. SL22 late readings after an edit, KEEP INK, a retry and its undo are ignored; a skipped request; an undone KEEP INK reads again. SL23 a reading for a line undone meanwhile is kept. SL24 without a reader: not recognised, RETRY greyed. SL25 the bar goes through the waiting lines; a note line picked by dwell. SL26 a stroke ends on the review bar. SL27 readings and decisions saved (v2), the strokes as written, only unread lines read on resume, v1 records load and are read, an unknown decision refused. SL28 the controls and bar drawn only with LINES, cached, with the dwell progress; the notes keep their text with PEN. SL29 the notes typeset (Unicode) and redrawn only when a line changes; a new stroke redraws the zone only. SL30 end to end in `draw` with a fake reader: DONE, the reading while the frames go on, OK by dwell, report, board file, `data/ai/lines/`.

**Validation of the two-text prompt (2026-10-27, §13.2), in short:** on the 22 distinct lines the literal text was exact on 19 (run 1) and 18 (run 2), CER 2.0 % / 2.5 %, against 20 and 1.0 % for Phase 0's literal-only prompt on the same images; the corrected text was exact on 19 of 22 in both runs and differed from the literal text on 0 of 24 lines in run 1 and 1 in run 2. Of the two real misspellings, the literal text kept KRITGYA but wrote MORNIG as MORNING: the literal text is not a reliable record of a misspelling (then the bar shows no "(written: ...)"). 24 of 24 lines readable; 0.5–3.4 s per line. The difference to Phase 0 is 1–2 lines (one writer, 24 lines): not significant either way. The prompt was not changed after the validation (no tuning on these lines); options for the user in §13.2.

**Physical test for Phase 2 (proposed 2026-10-27; to be fixed with the user before the run).** `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --seconds 900 --trace --save-every 1`. LR1 LINES: the corner guides, baseline, "Write here" and DONE are visible on the wall; write a word, DONE: the line appears at once in the notes as grey ink with "reading...", the review bar says "reading the handwriting..."; within a few seconds the suggestion appears in blue; drawing works meanwhile. LR2 OK: dark text; UNDO (blue again), REDO. LR3 write a line with a deliberate misspelling: note the suggestion and "(written: ...)"; AS WRITTEN (or note that it is greyed). LR4 EDIT: type a change on the laptop, Enter; EDIT again, Esc. LR5 KEEP INK on a line; hold on a note line to pick it; OK its suggestion. LR6 a scribble: "not recognised"; RETRY; EDIT it. LR7 7+ lines: scrolling, "k-m of n", the thumb, picking a line after scrolling. LR8 AI → SUMMARIZE with LINES while a line is read; drawing goes on; with the panel still open, write a line and press DONE, then OK / EDIT on it, where they now stand left of the panel (D65). LR9 ESC with one line still unread, then `draw --resume --seconds 120`: texts and statuses back, the unread line read. **Pass:** each step as described (the user's view, saved frames); nothing becomes text without a press; the board file's strokes unchanged; report `line_recognition`: fps while reading not below the recent runs' spread (43–56 fps); the user's judgment of legibility (notes, faint guides, 40-px review buttons).

**Limitations (Phase 2):** (1) the literal text can silently correct a misspelling (MORNIG), and the corrected text added a correction on 1 of 48 readings (WATERBOILS was not split): the teacher's check is the safeguard. (2) letter case follows the model, not always the writing (capitals read as "Ice is cold water", "science"). (3) readings are not fully repeatable at temperature 0 (1 of 24 lines differed between runs). (4) RETRY sends the same image and prompt: it helps after a failure (timeout, Ollama stopped), rarely after a misreading. (5) ~~with the AI panel open, DONE and the review buttons are under it~~ — fixed by D65 (below). (6) the tracker took +2.3 ms median (+7 ms p95) per frame while lines were read back to back in the replay; the live effect is measured in the wall test. (7) review buttons 40 px tall (toolbar 56 px); the guides' light greys chosen on a screen: both for the wall test. (8) without the AI (`--no-ai`) lines are marked not recognised (EDIT / KEEP INK still work; RETRY once the AI is on). (9) Edit Page (move / delete lines) not built; Phase 1 limitations (3), (4), (6), (7) stand. (10) validation: one writer, 24 lines, mostly capitals.

**D65: the AI panel and the Smart Lines buttons (user request 2026-10-27, after the Phase 2 report).** Request: DONE and the review controls must stay usable and must never be pressed through the AI panel; the AI stays in both modes; a minimal layout or panel-interaction fix, no redesign; nothing in tracking, landmarks, calibration, mapping or the camera changed; regression tests; full suite; no install, download, commit or push; stop before the wall test.
- The conflict: the AI panel (D51 / D61: x 824–1264, y 16–632, its answer area 258–632) lay over DONE (1064–1184 × 512–568), AS WRITTEN (partly), EDIT, KEEP INK, RETRY (the bar's right part, 340–392) and SCROLL UP / DOWN (916–956). D62's rule (an open panel covers what lies under it) already kept them from being pressed through it, so they were simply out of reach while it was open. Not chosen: a shorter AI panel (ending above the review bar its answer area would keep ~74 px, two lines), moving the AI panel to the left in LINES (it would hide the notes and the bar's text, so OK could be pressed on a suggestion the teacher cannot see), a narrower writing zone or a review bar always ending left of the panel (changes the approved design for everyone).
- The fix (`canvas/lines.py` `Controls`, `LinesLayout.beside`; `interaction/drawing.py` `lines_controls`; `canvas/render.py`): while a panel is open, each Smart Lines button it would cover stands just left of it, 16 px apart, same size and height: DONE at the right end of the uncovered writing zone (688–808 × 512–568), the scroll column at the right end of the notes area (arrows 768–808, track 754–760), the review bar ends at 808 with its buttons right-aligned in it (OK 272–336 … RETRY 704–800; the bar's text keeps ~200 px, shortened with "...", while the highlighted note line shows the whole suggestion). Buttons the panel does not cover stay in place; the PAGES panel covers none (the controls are the layout's own while it is open). The panel still covers what lies under it: nothing in it is pressed through, and a stroke reaching it ends there (D56); DONE inside the zone ends a stroke as any button does. Placements are made once per panel (the same button objects while it is open) and checked when the controller is built (no room left of a panel → a config error in `draw`). With no panel open, the D64 layout is unchanged.
- Tests (`tests/test_smart_lines_review.py`, 4; `tests/test_draw_tool.py`, 1): with the AI panel open no Smart Lines button, track or bar overlaps it (and it covered 7 of them in their own places), same sizes and heights, back on CLOSE, unchanged with PAGES, no room → ValueError; DONE, OK, AS WRITTEN, EDIT, KEEP INK, RETRY, SCROLL UP / DOWN and SUMMARIZE all work by dwell with the panel open; holding the cursor on the buttons' own places (on the panel) presses nothing at all, a stroke into the panel ends at its edge, a start on the moved DONE is refused and a line stroke reaching it ends there, after CLOSE DONE works in its own place; drawn beside the panel (with the dwell progress), the panel whole; end to end in `draw`: AI panel opened and left open, LINES, a line, DONE and OK by dwell beside it. Run against the old behaviour, 3 of the 4 new controller tests fail (the fourth, nothing pressed through the panel, held before too and guards it).
- Limitations: while the AI panel is open the bar's text is short (the note line carries the suggestion; CLOSE to see "(written: ...)"); DONE and the scroll column then lie over the right end of the writing zone and the notes (ink there is hidden under them while the panel is open; the zone and notes under the panel were hidden already); the buttons move when the panel opens or closes (the cursor is then on the toolbar or the panel, so no dwell is cut short). Wall test: the moved buttons are reachable and legible (to add to LR8).

**D66: fixes after wall test run 1 (user: "fix all the issues", 2026-10-27).** The run (§13.2) showed: (a) 3 of 5 LINES strokes refused although the fingertip was already in the zone — the hand rose into it while pointing and the engine's 7 entry frames (D49) started the stroke 13–43 px below the zone's bottom edge, which the Phase 1 rule refused until the hand opened (up to ~8 s lost); (b) one line stroke wandered from the zone up into the notes and down past the toolbar (its line read "T?"); (c) the guides and greyed buttons barely visible on the wall; (d) a CLEAR with LINES (nothing in the line being written) did nothing, silently, while the page had ink.
- `interaction/drawing.py`: with LINES, ink is made only where `_writes_line_at` (inside the zone, not on a button or panel there). A stroke starting anywhere else (below the zone, the toolbar band, a button, no point) waits (`blocked` "zone": the slashed ring and the hint, no ink) and begins (`_begin`) at the first point inside; a line stroke leaving the zone ends there (committed as at a pen lift) and waits again; DONE and the review buttons pressed while pointing leave the pointing waiting (`_pause_line_stroke`), so pointing back into the zone writes the next line; leaving LINES while waiting keeps D56 (nothing until the lift). PEN, ERASER, MOVE, SELECT and D56 unchanged. UNDO / REDO / CLEAR with LINES and nothing to act on show a message (`_lines_nothing`). Counters `line_starts_outside_zone`, `line_strokes_entered_zone`, `line_strokes_left_zone`, `lines:nothing_to_*`; outcomes `outside_zone`, `left_zone` (D55 diagnostics count them as any outcome).
- `canvas/lines.py`, `canvas/text.py`, `canvas/render.py`: corner guides 115 grey, 3 px, 34 px; baseline 165; "Write here" 24 px, 110; the idle baseline / ink 200 / 160; buttons outlined 2 px (100, greyed 170), labels 40 (greyed 160), arrows filled with the label colour; scroll track 200, thumb 120; review bar edge darker; the zone hint "Not drawing here: move your fingertip into the writing area to write".
- Replay of run 1's trace through the new controller (scratch `d66_replay.py`, the engine fed the traced shapes and cursor): the 3 refused attempts draw from where they entered (73.9 s from (113, 584), 83.4 s from (101, 542), 103.5 s from (530, 589): 38, 67 and 367 points); the one started in the notes area and never entering draws nothing; the wandering stroke is cut to its pieces inside the zone; 0 of 1,560 line-stroke points outside the zone.
- Tests: `tests/test_smart_lines.py` 5 new (rising into the zone from below and from the toolbar band, a start with no point, PEN keeps D56; leaving and coming back, nothing beyond the right end, each piece its own undo; DONE pressed while pointing then writing on, leaving LINES while waiting; the messages, none with PEN or when there is something to act on; the guides dark enough — fails with the old colours), 2 updated (a start in the notes area now writes from where it enters the zone; a stroke reaching DONE ends where it leaves the zone); `tests/test_smart_lines_review.py` 3 updated (the review bar, the AI panel and DONE beside it end a line stroke at the zone's edge; the baseline's new grey).
- Not changed by D66, still open: the two-text prompt (R40, the user's choice); flaky timing tests on a busy laptop (R41).

**D67: the reader's warm-up and darker note colours (user request after wall test run 2, 2026-10-29).** Run 2 (§13.2): the first line took 22.3 s to read, the next 1.7 s; Ollama had updated itself to 0.40.12 that morning and its log showed the first image request's prompt processed at 60 tokens/s instead of ~900–1,000 (R43). Suspected: a one-time preparation of the GPU code after the update — **a hypothesis, not proven**. Also seen: the blue suggestion and the grey ink of a line being read hard to see on the wall.
- `ai/recognize.py`: `warm_up_png()` (640 × 160 px white with one smooth synthetic stroke, ~8.6 KB PNG; ~400 image tokens, ~650 with the prompt, so the image path runs a full 512-token batch as a line's ~1,300 tokens do, at a fraction of the cost); `LineRecognizer.warm_up()` (one `/api/chat` request from `build_line_request`, so the same model, prompt, answer format, token cap and timeout as a line; the answer discarded; {"answered", "error", "latency_s"}; never raises); `RecognitionWorker(warm_up=True)` runs it once, first, in the worker's own thread (`warming_up`, `warm_up_result`): a line submitted meanwhile waits in the queue, so the reader never sends two requests at once; nothing from the warm-up comes back from `poll()` or is saved; an exception becomes a recorded failure; `close()` sets a flag, returns after its timeout, and the daemon thread then reads nothing more. Each reading also records `waited_s` (its time in the queue).
- `tools/draw.py`: the reader made with `warm_up=True` (it starts with the reader, before the setup check, so it usually overlaps the start-up); trace / records `lines_warming`; report `line_recognition`: `warm_up`, `warm_up_frames`, `warm_up_processing_fps`, `first_reading_latency_s`, `first_reading_waited_s`, `later_reading_latency_s`; console lines for the warm-up and the first / later reading times. The AI panel's own model load at start (D52) unchanged.
- `canvas/lines.py`: `SUGGESTION` BGR (190, 95, 25) → (160, 60, 10) (luminance 87 → 57, still blue); `READING_INK` 150 → 105 grey (also the "reading..." label, which was 125); black handwriting 25–30, so the three stay apart. Nothing else in the layout or controls.
- Measured against the real Ollama (no wall): the warm-up request answered in 1.47 s (the model probably reloaded after 30 idle minutes), then 0.26 s, 0.26 s.
- Limitations: whether the warm-up removes the slow first reading is to be measured in run 3 (the report's first line's time and queue wait): the 22 s case could not be reproduced (it happened once, after an update), and if a first-request cost depends on the request's size, the smaller warm-up image may not cover all of it. A line finished during a slow warm-up waits for it (its wait is recorded). The warm-up costs one small request per session even when no line is written; with the AI off there is no reader and no warm-up.
- Tests: `tests/test_smart_lines_review.py` 5 new (the warm-up runs once in the background before any line, the caller not waiting, a line waits behind it, nothing comes back or is saved from it, none without `warm_up`; a failed warm-up recorded and lines still read; closing during it returns promptly and reads nothing more; the real request is a line's request with the app's small image, every transport failure answered without an exception; the suggestion and reading ink dark and distinct, drawn so — fails with the old colours), 3 assertions now use the colour constant instead of the old blue's numbers; `tests/test_draw_tool.py`: the end-to-end Smart Lines test with a warm-up (once, frames > 30 fps during it, no line from it, the report's fields), the AI-on-by-default test (the warm-up starts with the session), and `run_tool` gives `draw` an offline reader unless a test brings its own (the warm-up must not reach a real Ollama in tests).

**D71: the Lines page in the look of the user's mockup (user, 2026-10-29: "given me like this lines page make changes to make exact like this", an image of the page).** The mockup kept D64's layout (notes at the top, the review bar, the writing zone with DONE, the toolbar) and changed its look; proposed with the differences below and approved ("go").
- `canvas/lines.py`: the notes area a framed rounded panel, almost the canvas wide (`[smart_lines] notes` 40–900 → 32–1172 px, y 48–324); the rows centred in it, 44 px high (`line_height` 40 → 44: 5 rows shown instead of 6), the text Segoe UI Semibold in dark navy (INK RGB 22, 38, 60), the line under review on a rounded light-blue row with the blue dot. The scroll column (new `Controls.column`, 66 px) right of the notes: SCROLL UP / DOWN 50 × 50 with filled triangles (blue when they can act), the track with the part shown in blue, a counter "last line shown / all lines" (replaces "k-m of n" in the notes). The review bar 60 px high (`BAR_GAP` 60 → 68), as wide as the notes and the column, its buttons 46 px high and rounded; OK filled blue with a white label (grey-filled when it cannot act), the others white with a navy edge and label. The zone (`zone` 40–1040 → 32–1088) with a light dashed frame along its edges inside the corner guides (darker, 4 px, 38 px), the baseline darker (165 → 125); "Write here" inside the zone at its top left until the first stroke (it was below the zone). DONE 132 × 60, centred on the zone's height (was low at its right), filled blue when there is a line to keep, grey-filled when not.
- `canvas/render.py`: the toolbar band typeset once per state (tool, colour, width, PAGES open) and copied over each frame: rounded white buttons, the colours and the widths each in a rounded group, the tool / colour / width in use (and PAGES while open) outlined purple (RGB 125, 90, 220; was magenta and square), labels Segoe UI Semibold 15 px (the largest that fits every 61-px button; was Hershey); the button places unchanged, so pressing is unchanged. The page label ("Page 1 of 1") in a rounded grey pill, typeset once per label.
- `interaction/drawing.py`: `_notes_right()`: while the AI or PAGES panel lies over the notes, they end 12 px left of it and of the scroll column moved beside it (D65: the PAGES panel now covers the column, so it moves too); the text is cut with "..." there instead of running under the panel.
- Kept from the mockup on purpose (stated in the proposal): the page white (the mockup's pale blue would dim the wall and show the light on the hand as a white patch again); the blue the darker one of D67 (the mockup's brighter blue was too light on the wall in run 2); the zone at the same height (hand tracking is best at the middle); buttons that cannot act stay greyed (AS WRITTEN when it equals OK; the arrows when there is nothing to scroll; the mockup showed them active).
- Cost: a frame with nothing changing renders in 0.72 ms median (p95 0.92; scratch `render_states.py`, the toolbar copied, the notes and controls cached); typesetting happens only when a state changes.
- Tests: `tests/test_smart_lines.py`, `tests/test_smart_lines_review.py`, `tests/test_canvas.py` updated for the new layout (5 rows, DONE's place, the column, the bar's height, the dashed frame, "Write here" inside, the counter, the PAGES panel now moving the column); 4 new in `tests/test_smart_lines_review.py`: "Write here" until the first stroke and back after undo / DONE; OK and DONE filled blue when they can act and grey-filled when not; the toolbar typeset once per state, the tool, colour and width in use outlined purple, the hold-to-press progress each frame; the notes end at an open panel and the page label's pill.
- To judge on the wall: whether the light greys (the dashed frame, the panels' edges, the bar's fill, the toolbar's groups) are visible, the toolbar labels' size, the counter's legibility.
- After the wall check of 20:25 (user "go"): DONE and OK greyed are white with a grey edge and label again (the grey fill hid the grey label: `OFF_FILL` removed); the counter and "Line n:" dark (`SECONDARY` RGB 50, 60, 75; "Line n:" in the semibold label font); the page label's text navy and 20 px (was 18, RGB 58, 65, 75); the toolbar's selection purple darker and 4 px (RGB 125, 90, 220 → 105, 50, 190; was 3 px). Tests: the D71 button test now checks the greyed DONE white with its grey label and edge, and "Line 1:" and the counter in `SECONDARY`.

**Wall test run 3 checklist (D64–D67; the steps not yet run, criteria from LR2–LR9 above and D66).** Command: `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --seconds 900 --trace --save-every 1` (run with `--no-recalibrate`, so a moved setup is reported for the user's approval first); afterwards `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --resume --seconds 120`. Each step: PASS / FAIL / NOT TESTED, with observations.
1. OK, UNDO, REDO (LR2): a line → its suggestion → OK: the note turns to dark text; UNDO: back to the blue suggestion; REDO: dark text again.
2. AS WRITTEN (LR3): a line with a deliberate misspelling → note the suggestion and "(written: ...)"; AS WRITTEN: the note shows the literal reading; or note that AS WRITTEN is greyed (the literal reading equals the suggestion — possible, R40).
3. EDIT (LR4): EDIT, type a change on the laptop, Enter: the note shows the typed text; EDIT again, Esc: unchanged.
4. Pick a note line (LR5): hold the cursor on an earlier note line: the review bar shows that line ("Line n: ...") and the line is highlighted; OK its suggestion.
5. Scrolling (LR7): 7+ lines: the notes scroll to the newest, the counter "m / n" (D71) and the thumb shown; SCROLL UP / DOWN; pick a line after scrolling.
6. The AI panel (LR8, D65): AI → SUMMARIZE while a line is read; drawing goes on; with the panel still open, write a line, DONE, then OK (or EDIT) where they stand left of the panel; CLOSE.
7. Empty CLEAR (D66): with LINES and nothing in the writing area, CLEAR: the message "With LINES, CLEAR clears only the line being written, and it is empty. To clear the page, choose PEN."; nothing changes.
8. Resume with an unread line (LR9): write a line, DONE, ESC at once (still "reading..."); `draw --resume --seconds 120`: the lines, their texts and states back; the unread line read again.
- Also: the first line's time from DONE to the suggestion (the report's `first_reading_latency_s` and `first_reading_waited_s`) and several later lines' times (R43); legibility of the darker suggestion and reading ink (the user's view).
- **Pass (LR, unchanged):** each step as described (the user's view, saved frames); nothing becomes text without a press; the board file's strokes unchanged; frame rate while reading not below 43–56 fps — to be compared with frames in the same hand state (run 2: without a hand in view the tracker searches, ~20 ms per frame, whether or not a line is read). Ambiguities noted for the user: "original text preserved" by AS WRITTEN means the model's literal reading (the handwriting itself is always kept as strokes), and AS WRITTEN may be greyed by design; the fps criterion as written does not separate the hand-in-view effect.

### 9.16 Writing over existing content: the light on the hand revisited (user request 2026-10-29; D68)

**Request (user, 2026-10-29):** writing over or near existing writing, the projected content lands on the hand as shadows, coloured marks or distorted text, confusing the writer and possibly the fingertip detection; wanted: a white or neutral area following the hand, the rest of the board unchanged, stored content never touched, tracking kept reliable; investigate first and propose; a small reversible experiment; tests; a physical A/B (effect off / on); no change to gestures, pen-lift timing, calibration or mapping; one local commit; not "fixed" before the wall test.

**Investigation (2026-10-29, reported to the user before any change):**
- *How content reaches the hand:* the projector shows the whole board each frame and the renderer does not know where the hand is; the hand intercepts the beam, so what is aimed at the wall behind it lands on it, shifted and bent (the hand is nearer the projector), with the hand's shadow on the wall (§13.2 2026-10-22). Purely optical: the stored board never changes (checked then by replay).
- *What the camera sees:* the real wall and hand, lit mainly by the projector (D34); the tracker reads the raw camera frame (nothing subtracted), so the same marks are on the hand in the tracker's image. Measured: hand found 100 % over plain white vs 62 % over ≥ 8 % dark content (dark room, 2026-10-22); G8 (busy content, room light on): 30.5 % with the light off vs 74.3 % on (§13.2 2026-10-23); R34 (SELECT loops broken over writing) has the same cause.
- *Already built:* D50 (`display/handlight.py`): plain white over the tracked hand's 21 landmarks (convex outline in projector px + 25 px), stretched ahead along its motion by the 0.26-s display loop, held 1 s after a loss; painted on the output frame only; ring cursor outside the light. Off by default since 2026-10-23 (user decision after G8 run 1, with a room light turned on instead); `draw --light`.
- *Which approach:* a white mask is the best-supported (G8: hand finding more than doubled; frames showed ink and text cut away around the hand). A neutral grey risks too little light for detection (D34: grey 40 → 0 %; 80–200 → 100 %): only a light grey (~200) would be worth trying if white glare bothers the user. Subtracting the projected image from the camera's view would need photometric calibration and exact latency alignment: not a small experiment.
- *Known limits and risks:* (1) only a tracked hand is lit: a hand entering or lost is searched for while covered in content (G8's weak point, R34); (2) the light follows ~0.26 s late (the display loop; the look-ahead stretches it along the motion, a stop briefly overshoots; no trails, redrawn every frame); (3) parallax: the camera beside the projector maps the hand to where it appears on the wall plane, not exactly where the projector's rays meet it; the margin covers a hand near the wall; (4) ink under the hand (including the fresh ink at the fingertip) is hidden while the hand is over it — mostly the hand's shadow anyway; (5) never run with what came since 2026-10-23 (Smart Lines, panels, documents, D58's setup watch); (6) G8's light-on p95 42 ms (> 33): search frames.

**D68: the small, reversible experiment (user approval "go", 2026-10-29).** D50's light unchanged in behaviour (no new mask algorithm before the measurements ask for one); off by default as before.
