- `tools/draw.py`: the `l` key switches the light on or off during a session (a fresh `HandLight` each time on: nothing held from before; ignored while an ASK question or a Smart Line is typed: then it is text); each switch printed and recorded (`light_ab.toggles`); per frame `light_on`, `lit` (a polygon painted) and `ink_points` (records; trace `light_on`, `lit`); report `light_ab.off` / `.on` (`light_state`): frames, seconds, fps, hand found (fraction of frames), hand losses and per 1000 hand frames, losses while drawing, draw frames, lit frames, ink points, inference and pipeline times (all frames / hand frames); a console line per state when the light was switched; the operator window shows the light's state. `report["light"]` stays the state at the start.
- Software checks: the light over everything drawn since D50 (freehand ink, Smart Lines notes, review bar, AI panel, a notice) is plain white inside the polygon, the rest of the frame identical, nothing stored changed (strokes, Smart Lines version and lines), the same frame without the light identical to before; the setup watch (D58) with a white light over a dark hand on dense writing, in the middle and near three corners: the canvas corners within 4.5 camera px of the calibration, no move reported; through `draw`: a stroke, then hovering over it with `l` pressed twice: the stroke white under the hand only while on and back after, no ink from the key, both states in the report and the trace; `l` while typing is text. Cost with today's renderer: update 0.07 ms + paint 0.14 ms per frame (median).
- Limitations (D68): the D50 limits above stand (only a tracked hand is lit; 0.26-s lag; parallax; ink under the hand hidden); the A/B is within one session, so the two states see different moments (compare like with like: hand in view, drawing or hovering); "hand found" counts every frame, also those without a hand in front of the camera.

**Physical A/B test for D68 (proposed 2026-10-29; to be fixed with the user before the run).** `.venv\Scripts\python -m invisible_canvas draw --seconds 900 --trace --save-every 1` (with `--no-recalibrate`: a moved setup is reported first); the light starts off; `l` on the laptop switches it (the console prints each switch). With the room as usual (light on or off as the user normally writes; noted). LH1 With PEN write 4–5 words across the middle (a full line), some in colour. LH2 Light **off**: move the open hand slowly over the words, then point and hover over them (no drawing), ~20 s; then draw over the words (cross them), draw close to them, ~40 s; several hand angles (flat, tilted, from the left and from below). LH3 `l`: light **on**; repeat LH2 in the same order and for the same time. LH4 `l` off and `l` on once more each (~30 s each: hover and draw), to see whether the order matters. LH5 With the light on: hover over the words and move the hand away: the white area should go within ~1 s and the words come back; move fast across the words: note trails or lag. LH6 With LINES (light on): write a line over the notes area's edge and press DONE / OK (Smart Lines under the light). LH7 ESC. **Measured:** per state `light_ab`: hand found and losses per 1000 hand frames (hover and drawing), losses while drawing, fps, pipeline median / p95 — compared like with like (trace segments of the same activity); unintended ink (strokes not meant: the user's note against the saved board); the saved camera frames (marks on the hand off vs on; the white area's position vs the hand). **Judged by the user:** whether the marks on the hand are gone, glare, lag of the white area, whether hidden ink under the hand bothers them. **Pass (proposed):** with the light on, no fewer hand-found frames and no more losses than with it off in the same activity; no unintended ink; fps within the recent runs' spread (43–56); no trail left after the hand moves away (frames); the user prefers it. Then the user decides whether the light becomes the default.

**D69 (user decision 2026-10-29, after the first A/B attempt, in which the light was never switched on): the light is on by default in `draw`.** `tools/draw.py`: `HandLight` made at the start unless `--no-light` (new); `--light` kept (no change); `l` still switches it; `report["light"]` the state at the start. Tests: `test_draw_light_is_on_by_default_and_no_light_turns_it_off` (default and `--light`: white over a stroke under the hovering hand; `--no-light`: the stroke shows), the D68 `l` test now starts with `--no-light`; every other `draw` test passes with the light on (34 of 34). README. Still to show on the wall: the white area on the hand with the light on by default, and hand found / losses against the 16:58 baseline with it off (67 % found, 6.4 losses per 1000 hand frames, 6 while drawing). The D50 limits stand: only a tracked hand is lit; ~0.26-s lag; parallax; ink under the hand hidden while it is there.

**D70 (user, 2026-10-29, after the D69 wall check: "the white flash is too big according to my hand … it flashes also in the background … tough to see what is written just next to it"): the light fitted to the hand.** Measured first: on the D69 session the look-ahead stretched the light a median 74 px while writing (13 % of DRAW frames at the 150-px cap; while hovering 34 % at the cap), and it stayed lit on 233 frames without a hand (the 1-s hold); on the recorded hands of 2026-10-22 (5,340 frames, all 21 landmarks; mapped with today's calibration), a hand-shaped outline alone was only 6 % smaller than D50's convex outline + 25 px: the stretch, the margin and the hold made the size, not the outline.
- `display/handlight.py`: the light is the hand's own shape: a capsule around each of the 15 finger bones with the finger's half-width (0.10 × the wrist-to-middle-finger-base length, 6–30 px) + `margin_px` 8 (D50: 25), and the palm's outline (wrist, finger bases) grown the same; moved by the look-ahead to where the hand will be when the frame reaches the wall, covering back only to `sweep_from` 0.75 of the way (D50: all of it), no look-ahead below 60 px/s (landmark jitter), the cap 100 px (D50: 150), held 0.4 s after a loss with 8 px more (D50: 1 s, 45 px). `hand_shape`, `finger_radius`, `contains`; `paint` takes a polygon or a list of them; the ring cursor kept at 38 px.
- Chosen on the recorded hands, each frame's light against the hand where it was 0.26 s later (when that frame reached the wall; the hand drawn with an anatomical finger width): D50 covered 100 % of it (median; p10 82 %) and hid 24,800 px² of wall around it (median); finger width 0.125 × length: 98 % / 16,400; 0.10: 95 % / 14,000; **the defaults (0.10, margin 8, from 0.75): 93 % (p10 64 %) / 11,500 px² (−54 %)**; a 4-px margin 88 % / 9,200; the cap at 70 instead of 100 px changed little. Scratch `light_fit.py`.
- `tools/draw.py`: `[` tighter / `]` looser: the margin ± 4 px (0–32), printed, in the report (`light_margin_px`: start, end, changes) and on the operator window; kept when `l` switches the light on again; text while typing. README.
- Tests: `tests/test_handlight.py` rewritten for the shape (fits a pointing hand, every landmark lit, not the gap between finger and thumb that D50 lit, 62 % of D50's area still; moves ahead and covers back only part of the way, none for a still hand, the cap; a short hold; painting a list), the D68 render test unchanged (passes); `tests/test_draw_tool.py`: `[` / `]` through `draw` (4, 0, 0, 4: never below 0) and as text while typing.
- Limits: a tighter light leaves more of a fast-moving hand uncovered for a moment (93 % median against 100 %); the shape follows the 21 landmarks, so a misplaced landmark misplaces its finger's light; parallax for parts of the hand away from the wall remains; the forearm is not lit. To judge on the wall: the size against the hand, the writing next to it, and the marks on the hand while moving.

### 9.17 Zoom on a document (user request 2026-10-30; D72)

**Request (user, 2026-10-30):** "when in pages from open with if i open any document there should be an option to zoom in and zoom out for the document for better readability add that option properly it should look good". Seen first: a portrait A4 PDF page fitted whole into the 1256 × 624 px window is ~441 px wide, so 12-pt text is ~9 projector px high: not readable on the wall. A preview (the test PDF at 100 % and 250 %, the bar in the D71 look) was shown with the proposal; the user said "go".

**Design (D72).**
- **The zoom bar**, only while a document is shown: a rounded bar at the bottom right just above the toolbar (341 × 62 px, inside the white margin), in the D71 look: a magnifier, ZOOM OUT (−), the level ("250%"), ZOOM IN (+), a divider, FIT. Pressed by holding the cursor 1 s, like the toolbar; a button that cannot act is greyed (− at 100 %, + at 400 %, FIT at 100 %). While the AI panel is open the bar stands 12 px left of it (as D65 does for the Smart Lines buttons); the board panel does not reach it. Never drawn on (a stroke starting on it is refused, one reaching it ends there: D56).
- **Zoom levels** (of the page fitted whole): 100, 125, 150, 200, 250, 300, 400 %. A zoom keeps the window's centre on the same place of the page; FIT goes back to the page whole.
- **The page at a zoom** (`Reference.display`): drawn larger (a PDF page rendered by PDFium at least that large, an image enlarged) and seen through the window inside the 12-px white margin, so the margin stays white for the setup watch (D58). At 100 % the page is shown by the same code as before, byte for byte (tested against D60's own computation).
- **Where the page is** (`PageView`): page coordinates are the wall px of the page fitted whole (the coordinates the annotations were already kept in); wall = (p − page corner) × zoom + origin. Where the zoomed page is narrower than the window it is centred; where it is larger it can be moved only as far as it still covers the window. A page turn keeps the zoom and starts at the top of the next page (centred across); SHOW BOARD / SHOW DOC keep the zoom and place; both are saved with the board (`to_dict`: `zoom`, `origin`; a board saved before D72 opens at 100 %).
- **The annotations stay on the page:** the controller converts a pointing stroke's wall points to page coordinates (`page_view`) before any tool uses them, so the pen, eraser, MOVE and SELECT act on the page's own layer as before; the pen's width, the eraser's reach and MOVE's 12-px hit tolerance are divided by the zoom, so they look and act the same on the wall as they are drawn (a line drawn at 250 % is 2.5× thinner at 100 %, as in a PDF reader). The renderer draws the layer, the stroke being drawn, the selection and the SELECT loop at the zoom, inside the window only. Diagnostics keep wall points.
- **Seeing the rest of a zoomed page:** with MOVE, a stroke that starts on the zoomed page where there is no ink drags the page (wall px, follows the fingertip, clamped; not an undo step; `doc:pans`); one that starts on ink moves the ink as before. At 100 % MOVE where there is no ink does nothing, as before. A slim scroll bar (the Lines track and blue thumb) along the right side and, when wider, the bottom shows which part of the page is seen, short of the zoom bar.
- **Report / trace:** `actions` `press:zoom:*`, `doc:zoom_in` / `_out` / `_fit`, `zoom_limit`, `doc:pans`; `reference.zoom_at_end`; trace `zoom` per frame while a document is shown.

**Not changed:** hand tracking and its thresholds, gesture classification, the engine (pen-lift delay), the camera-to-wall mapping and calibration (the zoom is a page-to-wall transform for the document only), the whiteboard and Smart Lines, the AI (it still reads the whole page, D61: reading only the zoomed part was offered and not chosen), the toolbar.

**Measured in software (2026-10-30, this laptop on battery, 69 %):** a steady frame 1.19 ms at 100 % and 1.35 ms at 400 % (the test PDF; 5 annotation strokes); a zoom press with its first frame 6–19 ms (the PDF re-rendered at the new size, once); a frame while the page is dragged ~5.3 ms median, 6.7 ms max (the page composed and the annotations redrawn each frame).

**Tests:** `tests/test_document_zoom.py` (13): 100 % byte-identical to D60's fitting (an image and a PDF; also after zooming and FIT); the steps keep the window's centre and stop at the limits; the zoomed page is the page drawn larger, the margin and the toolbar band white; dragging clamped, a narrow page centred; a page turn keeps the zoom and starts at the top; ink drawn at 200 % on the same place of the page at 100 %, width halved, on the document's layer; the eraser's and MOVE's reach on the wall, MOVE on ink at 200 % moves it half as far on the page, undo; MOVE where no ink is drags the page, not an undo step, diagnostics in wall points; SELECT while zoomed; the bar only with a document, pressed by holding, greyed at the limits, a stroke refused on it and ended at it, beside the AI panel, not moved by the board panel, gone on SHOW BOARD; the greyed glyphs and the scroll bars; saved and restored, an old board at 100 %, a bad zoom ignored; the setup watch over a dark page at 400 %. `tests/test_draw_tool.py`: the document test checks `zoom_at_end` and the trace's `zoom`. Full suite: 706 passed, 22 skipped, 1 failed (23:09–23:18, 8 min 56 s): the known intermittent R36 (`test_landmark_diagnostics.py`, a time-rounding comparison; that file re-run alone: 22 passed, 20 skipped).

**Wall test (protocol fixed 2026-10-30, before any run):** `draw --seconds 600 --trace --save-every 1`; a PDF with small text.
- Z1 OPEN FILE (or `o`), the PDF: the zoom bar at the bottom right, "100%", − and FIT greyed.
- Z2 hold + four times: 125, 150, 200, 250 %; the text readable; the scroll bar at the right.
- Z3 MOVE, drag the page up from a part with no ink: the page follows the fingertip; it stops at the bottom of the page; drag back to the top.
- Z4 PEN, circle a word at 250 %; FIT: the circle around the same word, thinner; + back to 250 %: still around it.
- Z5 ERASER on part of the circle at 250 %; UNDO.
- Z6 PAGES → DOC >: the next page at 250 %, from the top; < DOC.
- Z7 AI: the bar left of the panel; + / − work there; SUMMARIZE (reads the whole page); CLOSE.
- Z8 ESC; `draw --resume --seconds 60`: the document at its page, zoom and place.
Recorded, not gated: the user's view of the look and readability; hand finding and fps while zoomed and while dragging (trace `zoom`); setup-watch reports (0 expected).

**Limits (known):**
- Z-L1 Moving around a zoomed page needs MOVE (1 s to choose it, 1 s back to PEN); there is no scroll by buttons.
- Z-L2 A zoom press is one long frame (6–19 ms measured on the test PDF; a heavy PDF page at 400 % may take longer); dragging costs ~4 ms more per frame than a still page.
- Z-L3 The page label pill and the zoom bar cover a little of a zoomed page (top left, bottom right); move the page to see under them.
- Z-L4 Ink drawn on the 12-px margin or beyond the page at a zoom is kept but not shown until the page is moved there or shown whole.
- Z-L5 More projected content on the hand (a zoomed page fills the window); measured in the wall test (as L2).

### 9.18 The AI on a selected part of a document (user request 2026-10-31; D74)

**Request (user, 2026-10-31):** "if i open a document i want the select button to work if i want to use ai for anything in document i could select that part and use". Before: SELECT on a document selected the annotation strokes inside the loop, and the AI read the whole page whatever was selected (D61). The proposal was shown first; the user said "go".

**Design (D74).**
- On a document, a SELECT loop chooses a **part of the page**: the box around the whole loop as drawn (`selection.loop_region`; a box under the stroke selection's minimum area, 400 px², is a slip; after the wall test of 01:03 not closed first at its tightest point (D53), which kept slivers of open loops), cut to the page, in the page's own coordinates (D72), for the page it was drawn on (`DrawingController.doc_region`, `region`). Ink is not selected on a document any more. Less than 12 page px of it on the page either way (`MIN_REGION_PX`), a slip, or a loop off the page: nothing chosen (the whole page again).
- The AI then reads only that part: `Reference.ai_png(region)` with the region as fractions of the page (`region_fraction`): a PDF's part rendered by PDFium with its `crop` at the scale that makes its longer side `AI_MAX_SIDE` (1600 px; at most 6× the whole page's scale, `MAX_REGION_SCALE`), a picture's part cut from the picture itself (reduced only above 1600 px). The page's own content, not the annotations (as D61). The request's context gets `region` (4 fractions), and the system prompt one sentence more (`SYSTEM_REGION`: the image is the part the teacher selected; "this page" means that part); a whole-page request is unchanged (no `region`, the same prompt, `ai_png()` called as before).
- On the wall: the part framed in magenta (3 px, 4 px outside the box), at the zoom and inside the page's window (D72); the panel's title "AI on part of PDF page 2 of 3" / "AI on part of the image" (a title too wide for the panel is now drawn smaller), its menu line "Choose what the AI should do with the selected part."
- Dropped by a page turn, SHOW BOARD, CLOSE DOC, another document and CLEAR; kept by a zoom (page coordinates). The report: `actions` `doc:selections`, `doc:selection_none`; `selections` entries with `region` (page coordinates).

**Not changed:** SELECT on the whiteboard (strokes, D51 / D53), the AI on handwriting, tracking, the engine, the mapping, the zoom (D72).

**Tests:** `tests/test_document_selection.py` (8): a loop chooses a part, not the ink, the title and the log; the AI's image is only the part at 1600 px, of the right quarter of a four-colour page (top-left, then bottom-right), the whole page as before without one; a picture's part is the picture's own pixels; a slip and a loop off the page choose nothing, a loop half off the page is cut to it; a page turn, SHOW BOARD and CLEAR drop it, a zoom keeps it; a loop at 200 % chooses the part under it; the frame on the wall at 100 % and at 150 %, nothing in the white margin; the prompt sentence only with a part. `tests/test_document_zoom.py`: the D72 SELECT test now checks the part chosen (it checked the ink selected). Full suite: 715 passed, 22 skipped, 0 failed (00:52–01:00, 8 min 21 s).

**Measured in software (2026-10-31, the test PDF):** the part around four lines of text rendered for the AI in 6.9 ms at 1599 × 479 px (sharp; the whole page is 1600 px tall).

**Wall test (protocol fixed 2026-10-31, before any run):** `draw --seconds 600 --trace --save-every 1`; a PDF with text.
- S1 OPEN FILE; SELECT; a loop around one paragraph: the magenta frame around it; AI: the title "AI on part of PDF page k of n"; SUMMARIZE: the answer about that paragraph only.
- S2 ASK about that paragraph; EXPLAIN.
- S3 + to 200 %: the frame follows; a new loop around a smaller part (a line, a word): it replaces the first; SUMMARIZE.
- S4 a loop beside the page (or a small slip): the title back to "AI on PDF page k of n".
- S5 DOC >: no frame on the next page.
Recorded: the user's judgment of the answers against the selected text; the AI's time per answer.

**Limits:** the part is a box around the loop (a curved loop's corners come with it, and so does the finger's way to where the loop begins); the AI does not see the rest of the page (a question about something outside the part may get "not on the page"); a selected part of a small picture is sent at the picture's own size (not enlarged).

### 9.19 Voice writing (user request 2026-10-31; D75: steps V0, V1 and V2 built)

**Request (user, 2026-10-31, a detailed specification pasted in the chat):** VOICE WRITING MODE: a VOICE button; the microphone on the laptop's headphone jack; speech written on the board as clean text in a framed writing area (spaces, punctuation, word-wise wrapping at the area's edge, line spacing, scrolling, no overlap); continuous speech without duplicate words; voice commands distinguished from dictation (new line, new paragraph, delete last word / sentence, undo, clear current line with confirmation, stop / resume listening) with a switch for them; SELECT + DELETE of written text; voice text as structured text, separate from the ink, per page, saved and resumed, read by the AI; a compact panel (states, preview, pause / resume, finish, cancel, microphone choice); audio off the camera loop; local / offline unless approved; no change to the existing systems without approval; tests; a physical checklist; brain.md, README, focused commits, no push.

**Inspection (2026-10-31):** `sounddevice` 0.5.6 already in the venv (`requirements.lock`); Windows has the offline "Microsoft Speech Recognizer 8.0 for Windows (English - US)" (System.Speech); Ollama's model reads no audio. The jack microphone was not a Windows input (the driver's "Realtek HD Audio Mic input" pin recorded exact zeros) until the user set the jack to **Headset** in the Realtek Audio Console: then "Microphone (Realtek(R) Audio)" (MME, DirectSound, WASAPI), recording real sound.

**Step V0 — the engine, measured on the user's voice (the jack mic, 16 kHz mono; 10 classroom sentences, 105 words):**
- Windows' built-in recognizer (System.Speech dictation fed by the app over stdin; scratch `v0_sapi.ps1`, `v0_run.py`; no download): **13 / 105 words right (12 %)**, confidence 0.0–0.67 ("The capital of India is New Delhi." → "the capitol today is your pity"); speech then quiet (block rms ≤ 0.03). Not usable.
- **faster-whisper 1.2.1** (MIT; CTranslate2 4.8.4; installed in the venv with the user's approval, with onnxruntime 1.31.1, av 19.0.2, tokenizers 0.23.3, huggingface_hub 2.4.0; numpy unchanged) and the models `base.en` (141 MB) and `small.en` (464 MB) in `models/whisper/` (git-ignored). The reading recorded once (scratch `v0_reading.wav`, 150 s, louder: peak 0.22), cut into utterances at pauses by its Silero VAD (700 ms silence), each transcribed alone (greedy, no previous-text conditioning; scratch `v0_whisper.py`):
  - `base.en`, CPU int8, 4 threads: **104 / 105 words right (99 %)** (the miss: "date" → "data"), punctuation and capitals its own; **0.48 s per utterance** (median; utterances 3.8 s), 4 cores busy meanwhile; with **2 threads: 99 %, 0.57 s**, 2 cores.
  - `small.en`, CPU int8, 4 threads: 99 % too, 1.36 s per utterance.
  - GPU (`cuda`, float16): the RTX 5070 is seen, but `cublas64_12.dll` is missing (NVIDIA's cuBLAS / cuDNN for CUDA 12: ~1 GB more to download; not done).
  - The room: after the sentences Whisper also wrote other people's talk; the user's utterances measured −29.7 to −36.2 dBFS (90th percentile of 25-ms frames), the others −44.7 to −62.6: a level gate near −42 dBFS separates them. Three commands said together in one breath ("new line delete last word full stop") came out garbled in a sentence: commands must be said alone between pauses.
- **Decision (measured):** Whisper `base.en` on the CPU, 2 threads, utterances cut at pauses; text appears after each pause (~0.6 s of transcription after the 0.7-s pause), not word by word.

**Step V1 (D75, user "go" 2026-10-31, approving the design and its changes to existing systems) — built.**
- **Text** (`canvas/voice_text.py`, pure): a page's typed text is tokens (words with their own punctuation; `NEW_LINE`, `NEW_PARAGRAPH`), each change one step of its own undo history; `write`, `punctuate`, `new_line`, `new_paragraph`, `delete_last_word` (and the breaks after it), `delete_last_sentence` (back to the previous sentence's end or paragraph; a break said between the two sentences stays), `clear_line`, `clear`, `restore` (CANCEL), `delete_words` (V2). `VoiceLayout`: words left to right, one space, a word that would cross the area's right edge starts the next line (only a word wider than a whole line is split), one line height (1.45 × the text size), the view follows the end, `scroll` lines back; `render` draws only inside the area (Segoe UI 30 px, navy), the frame and the writing mark (blue bar after the last word) only while voice writing is on.
- **Commands** (`voice/commands.py`): only when the WHOLE utterance is the command (normalised; at most 5 words; a close match ≥ 0.85 for slips): new line / paragraph, delete last word / sentence, undo, redo, clear current line (said twice within 6 s), stop / resume listening, comma, full stop / period, question mark, exclamation mark. While paused only "resume listening" is heard.
- **Listener** (`voice/listener.py`): sounddevice (MME, 16 kHz mono; the device by a part of its name) → `Segmenter` in PortAudio's thread (30-ms frames; an utterance begins with 2 frames at ≥ `gate_dbfs` −42, keeps 0.5 s before them, goes on while frames stay above the gate − 8 dB, ends after `pause_ms` 700 ms below that, needs 8 frames at the gate; cut at 15 s) → a worker thread runs faster-whisper (`base.en`, CPU int8, 2 threads, greedy, no previous-text conditioning) and queues ("text", number, text, latency); a control thread opens / closes the microphone (`request`), so the camera loop only polls. Missing microphone, a busy one, the stream stopping (unplugged) and a missing model become a status ("unavailable" / "error" with the reason), never an exception in the loop. The model is preloaded in a thread when `draw` starts.
- **Controller** (`interaction/drawing.py`): VOICE (`Toolbar(voice=True)`, only with `[voice] enabled`) opens the voice panel (`canvas/panel.py` `VoicePanel`, 300 × 362 px at the top right: VOICE, the state with a coloured dot, the loudness bar with the gate marked, the microphone, then PAUSE / RESUME, DONE, CANCEL, COMMANDS ON / OFF, MIC, DELETE (V2), SCROLL UP / DOWN), one panel at a time (AI or PAGES closes it); refused on a document (and ended when one is shown); LINES gives way to it and ends it. Each utterance once (`voice_result`, a number seen before ignored): a command edits, else written at the end of the page's text (`Page.voice`), the view back to the end. While the panel is open UNDO / REDO / CLEAR act on the typed text (as with LINES); CANCEL restores what each page had when voice writing began (one step each). The typed text is drawn under the ink in its area (`background`, the image kept until it changes).
- **Board / config / tool:** `Board` saves the pages' text under the optional key "voice" (old boards load); `[voice]` in the config (enabled, device "Realtek", model, threads 2, gate_dbfs −42, pause_ms 700, max_utterance_s 15, commands, area [40, 60, 952, 632], font, text_size 30); `draw --mic NAME`, `--no-voice`; the trace `voice_state`; the report `voice` (device, utterances, texts, errors, transcription and end-of-speech-to-text times, every utterance and what it did, the words on each page, fps while voice writing is on and while transcribing). `pyproject.toml` extra `voice` (faster-whisper); `requirements.lock` the installed set (13 packages added, none changed).
- **Changes to existing systems (approved with "go"):** the toolbar's last group gets VOICE when voice writing is on (buttons 61 → 57 px; without it unchanged); the board file's optional "voice"; UNDO / REDO / CLEAR on the typed text while the voice panel is open; the renderer's toolbar band outlines VOICE while open. Not changed: camera, tracking, calibration, mapping, gestures, pen-lift timing, strokes, the AI prompts, documents, zoom, Smart Lines.
- **Measured (software, the user's recorded reading, scratch `v1_segmenter.py`):** the app's own segmenter + `base.en`: 92 % of the words with one threshold (a trailing "using sunlight" fell below the gate and was lost; "Let us begin" cut off) → **98 % (103 / 105)** with the 8-dB hold and 0.5-s lead-in (the misses: "A triangle" → "Our triangle", "eight" → "8"); 12 utterances; 0.57 s of transcription each (median), model load 0.38–0.42 s. The real jack microphone with the real model, 6 s, no speech: opened in 200 ms (hence the control thread), states loading → listening, a silent room at about −96 dBFS, nothing written, closed in 130 ms.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_voice.py` (38): text writing, punctuation, breaks, deletes, undo / redo, saving (board, an old board); wrapping at whole words inside the area, one line height, only a too-long word split, following the end and scrolling, nothing drawn outside the area; 16 command cases (commands and sentences that are not); the segmenter (pauses, a quieter voice, a click, a quiet sentence end kept, a long utterance cut); the listener with a fake microphone and recognizer (the named device opened through MME, each utterance once and in order, recognition off the caller's thread, poll and audio never held up, asking to open / close never waits, a missing / busy microphone, a missing model, an unplugged one: statuses); the controller (the panel, one panel at a time, duplicates, closed, commands, pause / resume, the commands switch, clear line twice, UNDO / REDO / CLEAR on the text while open and on the ink after, CANCEL, pages, documents, LINES, the panel pressed by holding and never drawn on); the drawing (the panel, the frame, VOICE outlined, the text kept after, the ink over it). `tests/test_draw_tool.py`: `draw` with a fake listener (VOICE by dwell opens "Realtek", 2 utterances: a text and a command, a duplicate ignored, the report, the board file's "voice", closed at the end); `run_tool` keeps voice off unless asked (the tests' toolbar). Full suite: 753 passed, 22 skipped, 1 failed (02:21–02:30, 9 min 14 s): the D57 test's stand-in Toolbar took no voice argument; draw now passes it only with voice writing on (the toolbar call unchanged without it); tests/test_draw_tool.py re-run: 36 passed.

**Fixes after wall test 1 (user "go", 2026-10-31; the user: MIC was only being tried; "delete, scroll up and down were of no use"):**
- **Names:** `[voice] vocabulary` (default "Pratik", "Jamshedpur", "Invisible Canvas"; the city is a guess from "Jamshek Boon", editable) given to Whisper as the text said before (`initial_prompt`). On the recorded reading with 1 thread: 99 % (104 / 105) against 98 % without; no hint word written where it was not said; the phrase heard before as "Bratikuma" became "Pratik".
- **Frame rate:** `threads = 1` (2 gave 23 fps while transcribing on battery): the reading 98 % with 1 or 2 threads, 0.86–0.87 s per utterance against 0.58 s (the fps effect to be measured on the wall).
- **The panel:** SCROLL UP / DOWN and DELETE shown only when they can act (`voice_buttons`; the panel ends after its last shown button, `voice_rect`, so the page is free below it); the state 22 px (was 17) in a darker green, the microphone line 16 px (was 13) dark, the title 21 px.
- The faster-whisper library's INFO line per utterance silenced.
- Tests: `tests/test_voice.py` + 2 (the buttons shown only when useful, the panel's shown size and the page free below it; the vocabulary prompt and its config check). Full suite: 756 passed, 22 skipped, 0 failed (02:53–03:03, 9 min 37 s).

**After wall test 2 (user, 2026-10-31): "when I say again that I mean this it should replace the previous sentence"; what COMMANDS ON / OFF is; does voice writing lower the drawing's frame rate?**
- **Frame rate, measured on the two wall tests' frames with the hand in view:** the tracker 10.4 ms with voice writing off, 10.7 ms with it on (unaffected); the projection's render 2.1–2.5 ms off against 4.5–5.6 ms on: the voice panel was copied onto every frame through its rounded shape with a boolean index (~3 ms). **Fixed with `np.copyto(..., where=)`: 3.85 → 1.21 ms per frame** (0.58 ms with voice writing off; synthetic). The remaining cost is the ~1.2 s of recognition after each utterance (26 fps then, on battery); with VOICE closed nothing runs (frames with voice off in those sessions: 48–49 fps, 56 with the hand, on battery). Committed (ad56866).
- COMMANDS ON: an utterance that is a whole command acts; OFF: every utterance is written (to dictate the words themselves).

**Step V2 (user "go", the same day):**
- **Corrections** (`voice/commands.py` `correction`): an utterance starting "I mean", "I meant", "sorry, I mean", "no, I mean", "correction" (commands on) replaces the last phrase written (`VoiceText.replace_last`: the phrase's tokens while the text has not changed since, else the last sentence, a break after it kept) with what follows, first letter capitalised; "scratch that" / "delete that" deletes it (`scratch`). One undo step each. With commands off "I mean ..." is written.
- **SELECT + DELETE of typed words:** on a whiteboard page SELECT also chooses the typed words shown whose middle lies inside the loop (closed at its tightest point; `voice_selected`, highlighted light magenta); the ink inside it is selected as before. DELETE (shown on the voice panel only then) or the command "delete selection" removes only those words (one undo step); any other change to the text drops the choice (the words' places move); closing the voice panel keeps it (for the AI panel).
- **The AI on typed text:** the source "voice" when typed words are chosen, or when nothing is chosen on a page that has typed text (`ai_source`; a selection of ink only stays "selection"; pages without typed text unchanged): the image is the typed text set again cleanly, all its lines (`voice_text.text_image`: 1,200 px wide, Segoe UI 34 px, at most 1,600 px tall), the printed-page tasks and system prompt plus one sentence (`SYSTEM_TYPED`; `client.is_printed`); handwriting and document requests unchanged. Titles "AI on the typed text" / "AI on the selected words"; the panel's menu line names the typed text. The real local model through the app's code: SUMMARIZE right (2.9 s), ASK "When is the homework due?" → "Homework is due before Friday, as stated on the page." (1.1 s).
- Tests: `tests/test_voice.py` + 13 (8 correction cases; replace / undo / after a command / scratch / commands off; SELECT + DELETE by button and by voice, the ink kept, one undo step, the choice dropped on a change; the AI on the typed text and on the chosen words with its prompt, the ink selection unchanged; a long text kept within 1,600 px). Full suite: 766 passed, 22 skipped, 2 failed (03:24–03:33, 8 min 48 s): the known intermittent R36 and R38 (cursor tool timing; their two files re-run alone: 43 passed, 20 skipped).

**The scroll column (user after the V2 wall test: "a scroll button for the mike writing"; the text had not yet filled the area, so SCROLL UP / DOWN had never shown):** the writing area's right 58 px hold a scroll column in the Lines page's look (D71): ▲ at the top, ▼ at the bottom (50 px, pressed by holding, greyed at an end), a track with a blue thumb showing which lines are in view (`VoiceLayout.up` / `down` / `track` / `column_rect`, drawn by `voice_text.render(column=True)`; the words wrap left of it, 818 px of text instead of 876). It shows while voice writing is on, and after it while the text is longer than the area (`voice_column_shown`); never drawn on (a stroke there is refused / ends, D56). SCROLL UP / DOWN left the voice panel (now 3 rows: PAUSE / DONE, CANCEL / COMMANDS, MIC / DELETE). Tests: `tests/test_voice.py` (the column: shown, greyed, the words left of it, pressed by holding, never drawn on, the thumb, kept after voice writing while the text is longer, gone when there is nothing to scroll; the panel's rows). Full suite: 769 passed, 22 skipped, 0 failed (03:58–04:06, 8 min 40 s).

**Wall test V2 (protocol fixed 03:12 2026-10-31):** as W1–W9, plus: V1 "The capital of India is Mumbai." then "I mean the capital of India is New Delhi." (replaced); "scratch that"; V2 SELECT around two words → DELETE on the panel; V3 the AI panel: SUMMARIZE on the typed text, then on selected words; V4 frame rate with voice writing on, compared with off (the render fix).

**Wall test V1 (protocol fixed 02:05 2026-10-31, before any run):** `draw --seconds 600 --trace --save-every 1`; the jack mic (Headset).
- W1 VOICE: the panel, "Listening", the microphone "Microphone (Realtek(R) Audio)", the loudness bar moving when speaking.
- W2 Say 3–4 sentences with pauses: each written about a second after its pause, spaces and punctuation right, wrapped at whole words.
- W3 A long paragraph (5–6 sentences): the area fills and follows the end; SCROLL UP shows the first lines, SCROLL DOWN back.
- W4 "New line", "New paragraph", "Delete last word", "Delete last sentence", "Undo", "Full stop", "Clear current line" (twice).
- W5 "Stop listening", a sentence (not written), "Resume listening", a sentence (written); PAUSE / RESUME on the panel.
- W6 Draw with PEN over the text while voice writing is on; UNDO (the text's last step), DONE, UNDO (the ink).
- W7 PAGES → + PAGE (voice writing ends), VOICE on page 2, a sentence; < PAGE: page 1's text.
- W8 Someone else talking in the room at a normal distance: not written.
- W9 ESC; `draw --resume --seconds 60`: both pages' text back.
Recorded: words right against what was said (the user reads a prepared text), the end-of-speech → text time, fps and hand finding while voice writing is on and while transcribing, the report's `voice`.

**Limits (V1):** text appears per utterance after the pause (about 0.7 s of silence + 0.6 s of recognition), not word by word; a command must be said alone; numbers as figures ("8"); the AI does not read the typed text yet and SELECT + DELETE of words come in V2; the typed text is on whiteboard pages only, not with LINES at the same time; the GPU is not used (cuBLAS not installed).

---

## 10. Decisions log

| ID | Date | Decision | Rationale | Revisit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | 2026-10-08 | Python 3.12 | OpenCV/MediaPipe/AI ecosystem; MediaPipe wheels lag behind new Python versions | At the start of P1, check MediaPipe wheel support |
| D2 | 2026-10-08 | Phase 1 rendering: OpenCV HighGUI fullscreen window behind a small `ProjectorWindow` interface | No extra dependency; enough for patterns and crosshairs; low latency | Before P1 drawing / P2 UI: compare PySide6 (Qt), pygame (SDL2), web front-end (3D) |
| D3 | 2026-10-08 | Calibration is a camera→projector homography from four point pairs, estimated with a normalized DLT in numpy | Planar surface; brief specifies four-point calibration; pure, testable, double precision | 1B adds N-point; lens undistortion if A7 shows edge error |
| D4 | 2026-10-08 | Accuracy is measured on **held-out** points, separate from calibration points | A four-point fit is exact and cannot show its own error | — |
| D5 | 2026-10-08 | Calibration stored as versioned JSON in `data/calibration/` (git-ignored), with history | Human-readable; specific to this machine; history enables the repeatability test | — |
| D6 | 2026-10-08 | Config in TOML via stdlib `tomllib`; unknown keys rejected | No dependency; typos fail loudly | — |
| D7 | 2026-10-08 | Displays, DPI awareness and DirectShow enumeration via `ctypes` | 150 % scaling breaks placement otherwise; real device names without new dependencies | — |
| D8 | 2026-10-08 | DirectShow is the default camera backend on Windows (MSMF selectable) | Usually more reliable control of resolution, MJPG and exposure | **Validated 2026-10-09** on the Brio 100 (D25, §13.2). MSMF measured worse: ignores the FOURCC request, opens in 38–44 s, ~half of its reads are duplicate frames (R22). |
| D9 | 2026-10-08 | Capture thread keeps only the latest frame; all `VideoCapture` calls happen on that thread | Low latency; `VideoCapture` is not thread-safe | — |
| D10 | 2026-10-08 | `src/` layout, `pyproject.toml`, `.venv` | Standard, isolated, editable install | — |
| D11 | 2026-10-08 | Phase N = tier P(N−1) | The brief's Phase 1 criterion matches P0 | — |
| D12 | 2026-10-08 | **Approved:** Python 3.12 installed per-user via winget; project `.venv`; Phase 1 deps = numpy, opencv-python, pytest only | User decision | — |
| D13 | 2026-10-08 | **Approved:** vertical, flat surface (wall/whiteboard/canvas). Keystone is not relied on for mapping; the projector is aligned physically. | User decision; keystone can change and silently invalidate calibration | — |
| D14 | 2026-10-08 | **Approved:** Logitech Brio 100 is the initial camera. No new purchase. Capabilities are measured, not assumed. | User decision | If 1A measurements show it cannot meet targets |
| D15 | 2026-10-08 | **Approved:** git initialized; commit each meaningful milestone; brain.md stays the engineering memory | User decision | — |
| D16 | 2026-10-08 | **Approved:** Phase 1 split. **1A:** manual four-point calibration first. **1B:** automatic calibration. Manual assisted calibration stays as a permanent fallback. | Reliability first; manual mode works without any detection | — |
| D17 | 2026-10-08 | **Approved targets.** Required: mean ≤ 5 px, max ≤ 12 px. Stretch: mean ≤ 3 px, max ≤ 8 px. Repeatability: mean corner variation ≤ 2 camera px. A 1-px repeatability rule is **not** a hard criterion. Never tune just to meet a target. | User decision | After 1A/1B measurements |
| D18 | 2026-10-08 | **Approved:** hardware diagnostics (all displays, resolutions, refresh rates; all cameras, resolutions/FPS; explicit projector identification) come before calibration | User decision; measure before building | — |
| D19 | 2026-10-08 | Phase 1A validation uses manual clicks on held-out targets. Automatic validation comes with 1B. | Keeps 1A free of detection code while still measuring accuracy | — |
| D20 | 2026-10-08 | The process requests 1 ms Windows timer resolution (`timeBeginPeriod(1)`) at startup | `cv2.waitKey(1)` otherwise sleeps a full ~15.6 ms tick; measured: waitKey dropped to ~2 ms | — |
| D21 | 2026-10-08 | Per-frame image work in GUI loops uses OpenCV ops, not numpy axis reductions | Measured: `np.max(img, axis=2)` on 1080p took 30 ms (slower than one frame) vs 5 ms with `cv2.split`/`cv2.max`; GUI frame age fell from 16.8 ms to 0.5 ms | Revisit with the P1 latency budget |
| D22 | 2026-10-08 | Definitions. **Sustained fps** = the slowest full 1-second window over the run. **Display→camera latency** = time from submitting a white frame to the first camera frame whose projected area is brighter than halfway between the black and white levels. | Makes A3 measurable and repeatable | — |
| D23 | 2026-10-08 | Calibration targets are exactly symmetric about their pixel; the ring is mirrored because `cv2.circle` is rasterized 0.065 px off-centre | No systematic bias in clicked or (1B) detected centres | — |
| D24 | 2026-10-09 | **Exposure is config-driven.** `[camera] exposure_mode = "manual" \| "auto" \| "unchanged"` + `exposure_value` (DirectShow units, 2^value s). **Phase 1 default: manual −5 (31.25 ms).** Set through DirectShow `IAMCameraControl` (ctypes), verified by read-back, and kept applied by the capture thread: checked every 0.25 s, re-applied when the camera stack resets it (R21). If the camera does not confirm, `CameraManager` raises `CameraError` (the diagnostic `camera` tool warns instead). Manual/auto require `backend = "dshow"`. | Measured: auto exposure with AE priority 1 caps the Brio at ~15 fps in dim light; manual ≤ −5 holds 30 fps. A locked exposure also addresses R7. OpenCV `CAP_PROP_EXPOSURE` cannot read back auto/manual under DirectShow (`AUTO_EXPOSURE` reads −1), so it could not verify the setting. No dependence on the driver dialog. | If the room lighting changes (−5 too dark or saturating); in P1 if fingertip motion blur at 31 ms matters (−6 = 15.6 ms also holds 30 fps, darker) |

No 60 fps mode exists. | After the latency measurement (runbook step 10) and the P1 latency budget |
| D26 | 2026-10-09 | `probe.configure()` sets width, height and fps first and the **FOURCC last**; a regression test pins the order | With `CAP_DSHOW`, every later size or fps change renegotiates the format without the FOURCC and the driver silently falls back to YUY2 (Brio: 1080p and 720p at 4.97 fps). Measured: FOURCC first → YUY2; FOURCC after size but before fps → YUY2; FOURCC last → MJPG. | — |
| D27 | 2026-10-09 | **A latency run counts only if it is valid:** every trial detected, and the black/white levels re-measured after the trials still straddle the threshold (and exposure still confirmed). Otherwise `latency` prints NOT VALID and exits 1; such runs are not recorded as results. | The 18:32 run printed a median from 4 of 20 trials (16 missed) without any warning. With a ~275 ms step and a 1.5 s window, a valid setup misses nothing (0 of 120 trials missed in 6 runs). A miss means the threshold no longer described the scene. Stricter, not looser: no threshold was changed. | — |
| D28 | 2026-10-09 | **Setup check before validation:** `validate` first has the 4 calibration corners clicked again; if they differ from the saved calibration clicks by more than the repeatability limit (A6 metric, `repeatability_mean_px` = 2 camera px), it stops and asks for a recalibration; nothing is recorded as a validation. The result is stored in the validation report. | The 19:59 A7 FAIL (mean 5.87 px, every error down-right) came from a setup that moved ~5 camera px after the 19:53 calibration, not from the mapping (test log). Nothing detected it, so it looked like a mapping error. The check does not touch H, the error computation or any threshold. | When 1B detection exists: detect the corners automatically instead of clicking |
| D29 | 2026-10-10 | **Phase 1B, first increment: automatic cross detection, diagnostic only.** `calibration/detect.py` finds the projected cross from its geometry (two fitted arm lines, centre = their intersection; the ring is never used), and rejects with a reason instead of guessing. `python -m invisible_canvas detect` evaluates it on real frames; `calibrate`/`validate` stay manual until it passes. **Acceptance criteria, fixed before the first hardware run:** 24 targets (4 calibration corners at 6 % inset, 20 validation points at 4 % inset), rendered as in `calibrate`/`validate` but without label text, each with a black background frame and 10 single frames. **B1** all 24 detected on the averaged frame and ≥ 95 % of single frames accepted, every rejection with a reason. **B2** no false detection: blank screens and two crosses in one search window are all rejected. **B3** stability per target: single-frame sd ≤ 0.3 camera px and max distance from their mean ≤ 1.0 px. **B4** click-free consistency: H from the 4 detected corners maps the 20 detected grid centres within the unchanged A7 thresholds (required mean ≤ 5 / max ≤ 12, stretch ≤ 3 / ≤ 8 projector px). **B5** agreement with the manual centres (only if the setup still matches the calibration, D28 metric ≤ 2 px): mean ≤ 2.0, max ≤ 4.0 camera px vs the clicked centres (clicks are quantised to 1.25 camera px and carried ~1–2 px human error, so this bounds agreement, not accuracy); otherwise B5 is not evaluable. Edge and interior targets reported separately. | Automating calibration needs a detector proven on real frames first; manual calibration stays the fallback (D16). | After the hardware evaluation |
| D30 | 2026-10-10 | **Phase 1C: automatic calibration and validation, explicit and opt-in.** `calibrate --auto` / `validate --auto` (manual stays the default, `--manual` explicit). **Calibration:** each corner is projected alone, detected (whole frame, black background frame, 10 single frames), and must pass a per-target gate (averaged detection ok; ≥ 80 % of single frames accepted; single-frame sd ≤ 0.3 px and ≤ 1.0 px from their mean, from B3). The 4 points must be ≥ 5 % of the frame apart (no duplicate), a convex quad in TL-TR-BR-BL order (`validate_quad`) with the projector corners' orientation (no swap or mirror). H is fitted by the existing `Calibration.create()` from the 4 corners only; a **held-out check target** at (320, 216), off every symmetry axis, must map back within `required_max_px` (catches a cyclically relabelled assignment, which convexity cannot). Saved with `method: "auto"`, same file format. **Validation:** the D28 setup check with detected corners (same metric and 2-px limit, not weakened), then the 20 held-out targets detected; same statistics and thresholds; report `validation-auto-*.json`, `"mode": "auto"`, per-point detection quality. **Never** a fallback to clicks, old points or guesses: any failure stops with a reason per target; nothing is saved or recorded. `detect` only uses manual reports as its manual reference; `repeatability` only compares calibrations of one method. | B1–B5 passed on real frames; automation removes the 1–2 px human click error. Manual stays as the fallback and the independent cross-check (D16). | After `validate --manual` on an automatic calibration; open question 7 |
| D31 | 2026-10-10 | **Automatic mode is the default** for `calibrate` and `validate` (user decision 7). `--manual` stays explicit; `--auto` is still accepted (compatibility). `calibrate --dry-run` and `validate --simulate-shift` now work without a flag and are refused with `--manual`. An automatic failure still stops with its reasons and names `--manual` as the alternative; it never falls back to clicks. Regression tests: default mode selection, `--auto` compatibility, explicit `--manual`, option conflicts, default commands never request clicks. | Phase 1C passed on hardware, including the manual cross-check (00:44: mean 1.32 / max 2.92). Automation removes the 1–2 px click error. | — |
| D32 | 2026-10-10 | **Automatic-mode setup-check limit: 1.0 camera px evaluated, NOT adopted; 2.0 kept** (user decision 8 asked for evidence first). New config key `[validation] auto_setup_limit_px` (= 2.0), used when the calibration is automatic; manual calibrations keep `repeatability_mean_px` (2.0); config refuses an automatic limit looser than the manual one. New read-only diagnostic `setupcheck`. **Decision rule fixed before measuring:** adopt 1.0 only if (a) a ~10-min no-movement series stays ≤ 0.5 px, (b) simulated moves ≥ 2 projector px are refused at 1.0 in every direction, (c) a real small movement is detected. **Measured:** (a) FAIL: untouched for 8.3 min the scene drifted steadily, variation 0.06 → 1.52 px (mean shift +1.30, +2.40; largest corner 4.46 px); 1.0 refused from 4.6 min (5/11), 2.0 never. (b) PASS: 1-px moves 0.49–0.84 px (pass), 2-px moves 1.08–1.49 px (refused at 1.0), 2,2 and 3-px moves 1.62–1.83 px (refused at 1.0, **passed at 2.0**). (c) PASS: the real drift was caught at 1.0. One of 44 corner measurements was rejected by the stability gate (sd 0.31 > 0.3 px). | 1.0 is a good detector, but on the current mounts the scene really moves ~2 camera px within ~5 min, so it would refuse almost every calibration older than that; 2.0 lets moves of up to ~3 projector px through. The root cause is the drift (R10). Not weakened: 2.0 is the existing limit. | When the mounts are stable: rerun the 10-min series; if ≤ 0.5 px, set `auto_setup_limit_px = 1.0` |
| D33 | 2026-10-10 | **Phase 1D: index-fingertip cursor with MediaPipe Hand Landmarker** (user-approved dependency change). `opencv-python` is replaced by `opencv-contrib-python` at the same version 4.14.0.94 (MediaPipe requires contrib; still exactly one OpenCV wheel, §9.4), pinned `<5` because OpenCV 5.0 is on PyPI and MediaPipe's requirement is unpinned; `mediapipe` 1.1.0 (Apache-2.0) and its dependencies added; `pyproject.toml` and `requirements.lock` updated. Model `hand_landmarker.task` (float16, version 1) downloaded from Google's MediaPipe model storage into `models/` (git-ignored), SHA-256 pinned in `vision/hands.py` and checked at load. VIDEO running mode, one hand, inference on a 960-px-wide copy. Landmark coordinates: normalized (0 = image edge) → camera px = n · size − 0.5 (pixel centres at integers), then the validated calibration H. Cursor states: tracking / holding (≤ 150 ms after a dropout, frozen) / lost (hidden) / outside (hidden); recovery jumps to the new position, never glides. Smoothing (One Euro) is optional and off by default. The automatic setup check runs before tracking. No drawing or erasing. | MediaPipe was the planned P1 hand tracker (D1). Swapping the wheel keeps one OpenCV and the same version. | After the hardware measurements (FPS, latency, accuracy, stability) |
| D34 | 2026-10-10 | **0 % hand detection diagnosed: the canvas was too dark; default canvas grey 40 → 128, brightness pre-check, `handcheck` diagnostic.** The pipeline was checked step by step and cleared: camera frame BGR uint8 1920×1080 → INTER_AREA 960×540 → letterbox 960×960 → RGB, C-contiguous, `mp.Image` SRGB; VIDEO mode with strictly increasing ms timestamps; thresholds 0.5/0.5/0.5 (MediaPipe defaults, unchanged); model SHA-256 verified at load. Positive control (official MediaPipe sample photo through the same `HandTracker`): found, score 0.96. Real hand: grey 40 → 0 %, 80/128/200 → 100 %. In a dark room the projection is the only light on the hand; at grey 40 the canvas region reads 23 against a camera black level of ~16. The MediaPipe NORM_RECT warning is unrelated (it also appears when hands are found). Accuracy test capture radius 4 → 8 px (measured fingertip scatter 1.7–2.4 px rms made 4 px mostly unreachable; the error is still the mean position; no acceptance threshold changed). | Evidence before change: brightness was the only variable that changed the result with the same hand, model and code. | When the room lighting or the projector changes |
| D35 | 2026-10-10 | **Accuracy test without wall contact: "on the target" = the fingertip's shadow on the target centre** (the target's projector ray passes through the fingertip; the shadow and the target are both on the wall, so the alignment does not depend on where the operator stands). Cursor and target ring stay visible. Per-target diagnostics (detection rate, first-detection delay, handedness confidence, hand size, capture frame). **Provisional criteria (user), not validated:** mean ≤ 5 and max ≤ 12 projector px over the 9 targets; no persistent edge-target detection failure; reliable loss/recovery; 60-s free-movement test passes. **Finding:** with a single camera the calibration is exact only on the wall; a hovering fingertip maps to where the camera's line of sight through it meets the wall, not where the projector's ray through it does. The gap grows with the hover distance and the camera–projector baseline and cannot be inferred from the fingertip image: no threshold or calibration change removes it. Homography, thresholds and targets unchanged. | Viewpoint-independent and measurable without contact; the only definition the current hardware can check. | When the parallax approach (hardware/algorithm/hover band) is chosen |
| D36 | 2026-10-10 | **Approved direction (user): camera closer to the projector lens; tiled acquisition; grey 128 + room light as the acceptance conditions; fingertip measured against the projected shadow centre with the cursor visible.** Implemented: tiled acquisition (`--acquisition tiled`, default) with one region track; the shadow-centre reference measured at each capture (`shadow_tip_near`), a run is INVALID unless every target's shadow centre is established; `--withdraw`; rows/columns. Measured: the camera could only move to 18 cm (from ~30); thresholds, homography and targets unchanged. Outcome: acquisition better, 60-s test complete; accuracy run invalid (1/9 shadow centres), parallax 39 px where measured. | User decision after D35 | When the parallax approach is decided (depth measurement or hover band) |
| D37 | 2026-10-11 | **Tracking audit (user request: cursor does not follow the fingertip).** Diagnosis (read-only, saved frames + reports): (1) the 2026-10-11 runs used `--acquisition full`, whose only reacquisition is MediaPipe's palm detector on the whole frame (192 px): on 854 saved frames with a detectable hand it found 55 % vs 95 % for the 720-px tiles, and 0 of 473 live-`lost` frames vs 52; its report showed 0 search frames because full mode never updated the statistics; (2) end-to-end latency ~290 ms is hardware (R4), processing ~13 ms; (3) One Euro beta 0.01 lagged 5.6–8.7 px while moving; (4) a fresh detection on a 640-px region around a search hit found the hand in 81.5 % (720 px: 89.5 %), and an unconfirmed hit was drawn for one frame; (5) the search hit and the region put landmark 8 on different fingers in 6.3 % of frames (R25). Changes: `HandTracker` returns a search hit only after the region tracker finds the hand in the same frame (its landmarks), `Region.around` min_side 720, `HandObservation.view/tip_norm/tip_in_view`, `AcquisitionStats` for both modes (frames, track_losses, tip_outside_view; full: frames after a frame without a hand are search frames), `HandTracker.action`; `CursorUpdate.reason/since_seen_s`, `CursorTracker.transitions/reacquired_after_s`; OneEuroFilter default beta 0.05; `cursor`: an extrapolated fingertip is not drawn, records carry the drawn position and reason, report `transitions`, `reacquired_after_s`, `motion` (lag, still jitter, transient jumps), `--trace` (per-frame JSONL, off by default). Calibration, thresholds, hold time, hardware and display settings unchanged. | Measured on saved frames and replayed trajectories (§13.2, 2026-10-11) | After the D37 hardware verification; R25 once traces show the cause of the live transient jumps |
| D38 | 2026-10-12 | **Landmark-glitch diagnostics, Phase A: observational only.** New pure module `vision/landmark_diagnostics.py`: per frame, in camera px, (1) relative motion = |Δ(landmark 8 − landmark 5)| between consecutive camera frames ÷ palm length |landmark 5 − landmark 0|, flagged > 0.25; (2) extension = |landmark 8 − landmark 5| ÷ |landmark 5 − landmark 0|, flagged < 0.43; combined flag = OR (no decision when neither is True and one is undecided). No decision (and a reset of the temporal baseline) on no hand, missing/NaN/None landmarks, palm < 2 px, a skipped camera frame, a frame after an unusable one, or a fresh search acquisition. Handedness is not an input. `cursor` runs it after the cursor update and only records it: trace `diag` per frame, report `landmark_diagnostics` (counts, no-decision reasons, episodes of consecutive flagged frames with start/end, duration, frames, peak motion, minimum extension, reasons). The cursor, smoothing, states, tip validity, mapping and the jump counter are unchanged (verified bit-identical on the four 2026-10-11 traces). | Offline analysis of the four traces (2026-10-12): thresholds from glitch-free frames (relative motion max 0.168, extension min 0.48 on 13,998 clean frames; leave-one-run-out 0.24-0.25 / 0.43-0.44); 9/9 labelled glitch events flagged within 2 frames, plus the sustained misfit at 21:24 74.1-77.1 s (25/52 frames); the old jump counter caught 2/9. | Phase B (treating a flagged frame like an invalid fingertip) only after recordings with deliberate finger bends, fast flicks and pointing towards the camera; user approval |
| D39 | 2026-10-12 | **Automatic calibration recovery after a moved setup (portability), validated before it is saved.** New pure module `calibration/recovery.py` and command `recalibrate [--dry-run]`; `cursor` runs the same recovery when its setup check refuses (`--no-recalibrate`: the D28 stop). Steps: (1) candidate from the four corners the refused setup check has just detected (whole-frame detection, D29; their old camera positions are never used), through the existing `associate_corners` (missing, ambiguous, unstable, duplicate, not convex, wrong order, mirrored) and `Calibration.create` (`method: auto`, same projector points); (2) held-out check target within `required_max_px` (D30); (3) the 20 held-out grid targets detected and mapped through the candidate must meet the A7 required limits (mean ≤ 5, max ≤ 12 projector px), as `validate` judges; (4) a setup check against the candidate after the validation must pass (automatic limit, 2.0 camera px), so nothing moved during recovery. Only then `commit_recovery`: refuses unless accepted and unless `current.json` still holds the refused calibration; archives that one in the history (never rewrites an entry); atomic write of the candidate. Any failure: the calibration directory is untouched, the step and reason are printed with operator hints, `cursor` does not start. Report `calibration-recovery-*.json` every time. No threshold, detector parameter or file format changed; no offsets; nothing simulated in the production path. | Root cause of the 2026-10-12 19:19 refusal (`setupcheck-20261012-191953`): a real move, not a checker fault. All four corners were detected (whole frame, worst single-frame sd 0.06 px) at +72…+77 camera px in x with ±8 px vertical change at the bottom corners (perspective), variation 37.26 px vs limit 2.0: the refusal is correct (the old H would be ~66 projector px wrong on the grid). What was missing was a recovery path: `cursor`/`validate` stopped and asked for `calibrate`, which saves after one check target without the held-out validation. Offline from the report: the detected corners form a valid quad and the moved canvas lies inside the camera image (x 231–1658, y 147–962). | Default-on recovery in `cursor` after the hardware validation (open question 13); whether to recover after a failed corner detection (not done: the same corners would be needed) |
| D40 | 2026-10-12 | **Hand-search budget: at most 2 search views per search frame, in turn** (full frame, tile 1..8, full frame, ...; every new search, at start or after a dropped region, begins with the full frame; first hit wins). Replaces full frame + 2 tiles on every search frame. Unchanged: detection/presence/tracking confidences, region tracking and patience, D37 same-frame confirmation, the tiles, the report keys (`search_views` now ≤ 2 per search frame). | `cursor-20261012-201357` (30 s, tiled): no-hand frames pipeline median 53.0 ms (p95 69.3) = queue age 15.5 ms + inference 36.0 ms; hand frames 11.6 ms. 382 views = 128 search frames × 3 − 2 (the full-frame hit stopped after its first view); 131 no-hand frames = 126 search misses + 1 tile hit not yet confirmed by the region tracker + 4 region-tracker misses (derived from the counters; no trace). Offline on saved no-hand frames, one view costs ~10.5 ms whether full frame or tile (palm detector, 192×192 input; preparing the image < 1 ms), so 3 views exceed the 33-ms frame interval: the loop fell behind (117 frames in 4.88 s, ~24 fps, while searching). The full frame cannot be dropped: on 1,347 saved frames it found a hand no tile found in 42 (mostly near the image edges or large hands); repeated every frame it mostly repeats its miss (the 27.6 s loss: 10 search frames, full frame missed every time, a tile found it). Estimate, not measured on hardware: ~21 ms of search per frame; each view recurs every 4.5 search frames (~150 ms at 30 fps, vs every 4 frames ≈ 167 ms for the tiles before), the full frame every ~150 ms instead of every frame. | Hardware comparison with the same 30-s run; if reacquisition gets slower, a search that starts near the last region (not done) |

---

## 11. Risks and known issues

| ID | Risk | Mitigation | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Python not installed | Step 1A.0 (winget per-user) | Resolved 2026-10-08 |
| R2 | Camera choice | Brio 100 chosen (D14); measure it | Resolved |
| R3 | Projector auto keystone or auto focus re-triggers and invalidates the calibration | Align physically, keystone off (D13); recalibrate after any change; drift detection (P3) | Open |
| R4 | Input lag and image processing in the Android projector | Game mode on, enhancements off; `latency` measures it. **Measured 2026-10-09:** display→camera 275 ms median in total. The camera path is bounded at ≤ ~194 ms median (exposure-step test), so the display path (Windows compositor + HDMI + projector) is ≥ ~80 ms, plausibly ~80–150 ms. Projector picture settings not yet checked (§2.3); A/B with `latency` is the next step. | Open |
| R5 | 150 % Windows scaling distorts window coordinates | Per-monitor DPI awareness (D7); projector display at 100 % | Planned |
| R6 | Meta Virtual Monitor is listed as a display | Explicit projector identification (§9.7); virtual displays excluded | Planned |
| R7 | Webcam auto exposure, white balance or focus drift as projected content changes | Lock via config/driver dialog if the Brio 100 allows it; measure. **2026-10-09:** exposure is locked by config and kept applied (D24); the Brio offers manual exposure −11..−2. White balance and focus are not addressed yet. **Gain is not locked by manual exposure (measured 2026-10-09):** it stays wherever auto exposure last left it, so projected white at exposure −5 read 143 in one session and 114 in later ones (after an auto-exposure session). Levels are stable within a session. Gain lock through config is a candidate follow-up. **Kreo (2026-10-19, D42):** gain is manual-only 0..15 and sits at 0 under manual exposure, which leaves the 60-fps image too dark (grey-128 canvas 5.7); white balance is auto (1800..10000 settable). A gain lock is now needed (open question 16). **Locked since D43** (manual 15, kept applied). White balance and focus: focus locked (D42); white balance still auto. | Exposure mitigated; gain, WB and focus open |
| R8 | Lens distortion causes error near the edges | A7 error map shows it; 1B adds undistortion if needed. **2026-10-10 (automatic, click-free):** residuals ~1.0–1.3 projector px mid-span (x = 345 and 934, pointing towards the centre) and 0.1–0.6 px near the calibration corners, a pattern consistent with mild lens distortion that a 4-point H cannot model. Well within stretch; N-point fit or undistortion would reduce it if a later phase needs it. | Watch |
| R9 | Surface not flat/rigid, or a glossy whiteboard creates hot spots | Rigid, flat, matte surface; check saturation | Open |
| R10 | Camera, projector or surface moves after calibration | Rigid mounts; recalibrate; drift detection (P3). **Happened 2026-10-09:** ~5 camera px between the 19:53 calibration and the 19:59 validation (cause not identified), failing A7. Mitigation added: setup check before validation (D28). Measured afterwards: corners stable within ~1.3 px over 3 min. **Happened again** after the 23:24 A7 PASS: by 23:46 every target sat ~(+65, −11) camera px from the 23:20 calibration (setup-check variation 53 px). Over the next ~3 min the detected centres moved ≤ 1.0 px. **A third time** between 00:07 and 00:29 on 2026-10-10: all corners +22.8…+25.4 camera px in x (automatic setup check refused the 00:02 calibration, variation 12.39 px). Afterwards a slow creep of ~0.8–1.7 px (mostly +y) between 00:31 and 00:35. **Measured untouched drift (D32):** 01:02–01:10 on 2026-10-10 the corners drifted steadily, setup variation 0.06 → 1.52 px (largest corner 4.46 camera px in 8.3 min); between calibrations 00:36→01:02 and 01:11→01:20: 0.94 and 0.98 px. Mapping error grows with calibration age; the stricter automatic limit is blocked by this. **A fourth time** between 01:51 and 01:59 on 2026-10-10, during the hand session: corners +76…+84 camera px in x with a slight rotation (variation 39.9 px); the setup check refused `cursor` (as designed), `setupcheck` reproduced it, recalibrated automatically at 02:00:32. **Fifth/sixth time:** between 02:00 and 14:05 the setup changed substantially (variation 71.35 px; the projection now fills 48 % of the camera frame instead of 67 %); between 14:08 and 14:32 a uniform drift of (−7, +3) camera px (setup check 0.14 → 3.71 px across the session). **D36:** camera moved deliberately (15:00); after the 15:19 calibration a steady creep: 0.04 → 0.30 px in 100 s (stability check), 0.72 px by 15:36. Still on a bottle. **Seventh time:** before 19:19 on 2026-10-12 the corners moved +72…+77 camera px in x with ±8 px vertical change at the bottom corners (setup variation 37.26 px, `setupcheck-20261012-191953`). **D39:** recovery is now automatic and validated (`recalibrate`, `cursor`); it does not fix the mount, and a setup still moving during recovery is refused (confirmation step). **Kreo setup:** moved ~(+185, −138) camera px between 2026-10-19 and 10-21; on 2026-10-22 00:23 variation 12.78 px and at 00:28 24.37 px (still changing; corners moved outward, i.e. the projection grew in the camera image: camera or projector moved, or projector zoom). **2026-10-26/27:** moved before the 18:42 and the 00:25 sessions (39.84 px), and **during** the 00:25 session (the projection ~85 camera px, the camera not; the cursor ~96 projector px from the fingertip until the end; the user's report). **D58 (2026-10-27): `draw` now watches for a move during the session and recalibrates** (the validated recovery); it does not fix the mount either. | Open (D58 detects and recovers in `draw`; `cursor`, `gestures` still only check at start) |
| R11 | Projected light on the hand degrades hand detection | **Inverted on this setup (D34):** the projection is the hand's only light; too little light, not too much, stopped detection (grey 40 → 0 %; 80–200 → 100 %). Default canvas grey 128; `cursor` warns when the canvas region reads < 25. **Remaining:** a hand reaching in from the unlit surround (left edge, top-left corner) has its palm in the dark and is acquired late or not at all (24 % of frames at the top-left target). **D35:** the main cause is the palm detector's input (the whole frame scaled to 192×192, ~10 camera px per detector px; a hand is ~20 px there, an unlit palm leaves only the fingers): on saved frames cold detection found the hand in 26/74 full frames vs 55/74 with 2×2 tiles, and 8/12 vs 0/12 top-left frames with tight crops. A ceiling light raised detection to 93.4 % (left column 77/100/100 %) with 2 tracking losses, vs 74.9 % and 25 losses in daylight alone. **D36: tiled acquisition** (offline: found 13/23 visible-but-lost hands vs 0/23, 0/202 false positives; hardware: first detection after activation median 1.84 s vs 2.24 s full frame incl. reach, 0 false positives in 30 s). Cost while searching: ~25 fps, capture→projector ~51 ms | Mitigated (1D) |
| R12 | Fingertip hover parallax | **Confirmed as the dominant accuracy error (D35).** Single camera: the hover distance is not observable from the fingertip, so the cursor (camera line of sight → wall) and the projector ray through the fingertip (where its shadow falls) disagree by ≈ hover distance × baseline / distance to the wall. Measured: shadow 25–50 camera px from the fingertip at the bottom row (night frames; 21–41 projector px); with the shadow on the target, cursor errors 25–55 projector px at the bottom row (runs 2 and 4); pointing from arm's length puts the shadow > 200 camera px away. Options: camera at the projector lens, hover-distance measurement (shadow, stereo, depth), or a hover band. **D36:** camera moved to 18 cm (closest possible, projector top unusable); with the shadow centre on the target the cursor was still 39 px away (T4), implying a ~25–35 cm hover. Baseline ≤ ~3 cm would be needed: not reachable with a separate webcam | Open (1D) |

## 12. Open questions

1. What is the projector's EDID name? It is filled in after the first diagnostics run with the projector connected and goes into `projector.match`. **Answered 2026-10-09:** `AML TV` [AML0030] on HDMI; the automatic rule identifies it. Not yet written into `projector.match`.
2. Which Brio 100 mode to use for Phase 1: 1920×1080 or 1280×720? Decide from the measured fps and latency (default for now: 1920×1080 MJPG @ 30). **Answered by D25 (2026-10-09):** 1920×1080 MJPG @ 30 validated; both sizes measured the same fps; latency still to be measured.
3. Physical projected image width in mm, needed to report errors in mm (`surface.width_mm`). Measure during step 1A.5.
4. (P2) Classic OCR, or a multimodal model reading a rendered image of the selected strokes? Which AI provider/model? **Answered 2026-10-23 (D51): Claude (Anthropic API, `claude-opus-5-5`) reading an image of the selected strokes; no OCR library.**
5. Does the WZATCO Yuva Go have a game/low-latency picture mode, and which of its settings (MEMC, noise reduction, dynamic contrast, keystone) change the measured latency? (R4)
6. (1B) What moved the setup three times (≈5 px after 19:53, ≈53 px after 23:24, ≈24 px between 00:07 and 00:29)? Until the mounts are fixed, every calibration has a short life (R10).
7. ~~(1B/1C) Should `--auto` become the default?~~ **Answered 2026-10-10: yes (D31).**
8. ~~(1C) Stricter setup limit for `--auto`?~~ **Answered 2026-10-10 (D32): 1.0 px evaluated, not adopted while the scene drifts; 2.0 kept.** Re-evaluate after the mounts are fixed.
9. (1D) How well does the fingertip cursor work with a real hand on this setup? **Answered for detection (D34, D35); accuracy limited by hover parallax (D35).**
10. (1D, D35/D36) How should hover parallax be handled? **D36:** moving the camera is exhausted (18 cm, measured limit). Remaining: a hover-distance measurement (dedicated shadow detection, a second camera, a depth sensor) or an accuracy criterion for a hover band. Geometry recorded in §13.3 (vertical offset after the move not re-measured).
11. (1D, D35) Operational definitions for the provisional criteria: "no persistent edge-target detection failure" (proposal: every target captured within 30 s and ≥ 80 % hand-found per target) and "60-s free test passes" (proposal: ≥ 90 % hand-found excluding deliberate exits, every exit recovered within 1 s).
12. (1D, D36) Which accuracy reference, if the shadow centre cannot be established in room light / at the bottom row: a dark room for the test, a physical pointer of known length touching the wall, or fingertip contact for the measurement only?
13. (D39) After the hardware validation of `recalibrate`: keep automatic recovery on by default in `cursor` (current; `--no-recalibrate` stops instead)? Should repeated recoveries within a short time (a mount that keeps moving) stop with a warning instead of recovering again?
14. ~~(D41) Kreo Owl 4K Pro mode~~ **Answered 2026-10-19: 1920×1080 @ 60 (user), D42.** Original question: 1920×1080 @ 30 as with the Brio, 1920×1080 @ 60 if it really delivers 60 fps (halves the camera's frame interval; latency matters for the cursor), or a higher resolution for calibration accuracy (more decode time per frame)? Measuring 4K-class modes needs them added to the `devices` probe list (`camera/probe.py`), which is not done yet.
15. (D41) Is the Kreo permanent or temporary ("for now")? Camera selection is by name, so going back to the Brio means restoring its `[camera]` settings (D24, D25) and recalibrating.
16. ~~(D42) Lock the Kreo's gain through config~~ **Answered 2026-10-19: yes, gain 15 (D43).** Original question: Lock the Kreo's gain through config (`IAMVideoProcAmp` gain 0..15, same read-back/keeper mechanism as exposure and focus)? At −6 the image is too dark without it; gain 15 gives white 145.6 (like the Brio's 114–143) and grey-128 54.4 (the Brio: 142). Hand detection will also need a brighter canvas or room light; measure with `handcheck` (D34) after the gain lock.

17. (D54, EXPLORE physical run 1) Should EXPLORE correct an obvious misspelling (the model read "FLIGT" and explored "Flight") or refuse it with the note (what the prompt asks for misspelled words)? The READ line shows the original letters either way. After the 2026-10-26 prompt change the model also took "Pengyien" as "Penguin" (before: "a Chinese surname").

18. (D49 / D56, smartboard mission Phase 2) Should an open hand end the stroke at once instead of after the 0.6-s pen-lift delay? The user's requirement "do not reconnect a stroke across an intentional lift" meets D49 (a user decision: interruptions < 0.6 s are bridged, other shapes and no hand alike). Over the 24 recorded sessions 8 of 1,634 bridged interruptions contained ≥ 3 consecutive open-hand frames (0.06–0.46 s); whether they were intentional lifts is not known from the traces. D56 does not change the delay (the toolbar ends a stroke at once regardless).

Resolved on 2026-10-08: Python install (D12), surface orientation (D13), camera (D14), git (D15), accuracy thresholds (D17).
